Browse through our galleries to find out.\n\n#### Souvenir prices\n\n![Banknotes in South Africa]()\n\n#### How much does it cost to send a postcard from Cape Town abroad?\n\n#### Water Sports Rental Prices\n\n#### Beaches in Cape Town\n\nFor a sun bed and a sun umbrella for the day, prices start at US$6 | ZAR100 | €5.4.\n\n#### How much is iPhone in Cape Town, South Africa?\n\n![iPhone X prices in Cape Town]()\n\n#### Pharmacy prices in Cape Town\n\n![Pharmacy in Cape Town]()\n\n#### Hair & Beauty Service Prices in Cape Town\n\n![Cape Town Massage Prices](data:image/svg+xml;utf8,<svg xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg' class='' width='3204' height='2460' viewBox='0 0 3204 2460'><rect style='fill: rgb(230, 238, 255);' width='3204' height='2460'></rect></svg>)\n\n#### Real estate\n\n![Sales in Cape Town]()\n\n#### Winter and summer sales in Cape Town, South Africa in 2026\n\nWinter sales 2026: December 26, 2025 till late January 2026\n\nSummer sales 2026: June 6 till late July – early August 2026\n\n### Leave a comment\n\nHave a question about prices? Bookings are for a minimum of two people and maximum of eight guests.  \n Cost R590 per person (includes a glass of cap classique)  \n Times Monday – Saturday, 10am, 10.30am, 11am, 3pm (seatings)  \n Sunday, 10am, 10.30am, 11am  \n Contact 021 876 2961, (/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#deacbbadaabfabacbfb0aa9eb2bbb2abbabbf0bdb1f0a4bf)  \n Where to find it Bowling Green Avenue, off Lambrechts Road, Franschhoek  \n Book through DinePlan [...] The beautiful thatched Cape Dutch manor at Grande Roche, with its wide shady verandah giving panoramic views over the Paarl mountains, is the perfect setting for an old-world morning or afternoon tea. Arrive to a sparkling glass of cap classique, then settle down to an abundant feast of savouries from beef croquettes and caramelised onion and gruyere tartlets to smoked salmon blini. Generous sweet treats include lemon meringue tartlets, Basque cheesecake, mascarpone and lemon zest cannoli, and chocolate delice accompanied by an enticing menu of whole-leaf and single-estate teas (or coffee if that’s your preferred cuppa).  \n Good to know Book 48 hours in advance.  \n Cost R595 per person  \n R695 (vegan or gluten-free menu)  \n Times Monday – Sunday, 10am – 3.30pm [...] Good to know Vegan and gluten-free menus are available with advance notice. Champagne can also be ordered to make your après-midi that much more sparkling. And there’s a pet-friendly tea (complete with a doggie menu), taken outside on the terrace.  \n Cost R650 per person (includes a welcome glass of iced tea or L’Ormarins Cap Classique)  \n R350 (children 4 – 11 years)  \n Times Monday – Friday, 11am – 4pm (seating slots each hour)  \n Saturday + Sunday, 12pm – 4pm (seating slots each hour)  \n Contact 021 483 1000, (/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#562433252237232437382224332533242037223f393825783b383e1634333a3b3938327835393b)  \n Where to find it 76 Orange Street, Gardens, Cape Town  \n Book through DinePlan",

[X](https://twitter.com/theinsideguide)\n\n\n\n[Cape Town](https://insideguide.co.za/cape-town/) / [Things To Do Guides](https://insideguide.co.za/cape-town/category/things-to-do-guides/) / The 18 Best High Teas in Cape Town\n\n# The 18 Best High Teas in Cape Town\n\nA sumptuous and time-honoured ritual.\n\nBy Inside Guide  •  21 October 2026\n\n![High Tea](https://insideguide.co.za/cape-town/app/uploads/2025/11/high-tea.webp)\n\nHigh Tea\n\n\n\n### Share\n\n* [WhatsApp](https://web.whatsapp.com/send?text=The+18+Best+High+Teas+in+Cape+Town%20https://insideguide.co.za/cape-town/high-tea/)\n* [Email](/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#ead5999f88808f899ed7be828fc1dbd2c1a88f999ec1a2838d82c1be8f8b99c18384c1a98b9a8fc1be859d84cc88858e93d7829e9e9a99d0c5c5838499838e8f8d9f838e8fc48985c4908bc5898b9a8fc79e859d84c582838d82c79e8f8bc5)\n* [Facebook](https://www.facebook.com/sharer/sharer.php?u=https://insideguide.co.za/cape-town/high-tea/)\n* [Pinterest](https://pinterest.com/pin/create/button/?url=https://insideguide.co.za/cape-town/high-tea/&description=The+18+Best+High+Teas+in+Cape+Town)\n* [X](https://x.com/intent/post?text=The+18+Best+High+Teas+in+Cape+Town&url=https://insideguide.co.za/cape-town/high-tea/)\n\nFor food that looks too beautiful to eat, and service that makes you feel like a VIP, high tea is one of those [thrilling experiences](https://insideguide.co.za/cape-town/things-to-do/things-to-do-in-cape-town/) that sparks joy and lingers long after. The ptissier’s art and chef’s culinary skill, combined with a connoisseur’s menu of top-quality teas, create a sense of occasion akin to being spoiled on your birthday.\n\nKnown as afternoon tea in old-school tradition, and made popular by Anna, Duchess of Bedford in the Victorian era, this lavish ritual is largely the preserve of luxury hotels (with the occasional delicious exception), offering visitors a leisurely and sophisticated way to while away an afternoon over animated conversation and clinking tea cups.\n\nPinkies, ahoy!\n\n## Mount Nelson, A Belmond Hotel\n\n![High Tea Mount Nelson](data:image/svg+xml;base64,PHN2ZyB3aWR0aD0iMSIgaGVpZ2h0PSIxIiB4bWxucz0iaHR0cDovL3d3dy53My5vcmcvMjAwMC9zdmciPjwvc3ZnPg==)\n\nPhoto: Claire Gunn\n\nAfternoon tea at this “grande dame” is a Cape Town institution. Settle into a table on the verandah or the sunny terrace overlooking the magnificent gardens. Tea sommelier Craig Cupido guides you in choosing from over 60 of the finest teas and infusions, including a blend created to celebrate the hotel’s 125th anniversary: rooibos, honeybush, mesquite and apple. The accompanying three-course feast is served on silver-tiered stands: first sumptuous savoury pastries and delicate finger sandwiches, followed by a basket of warm scones with vanilla cream, butter curls and strawberry jam. Take a breather and brew a second pot of tea to go with the sublime confections dreamed up by pastry chef Vicky Gurovich: she changes things up often but think delicious bites, such as a peach and tea choux bun, a passionfruit cheesecake, and dark chocolate mud cakes, in ways you’ve never tasted before. And then there’s a tempting table of cakes by the slice in the lounge (a warm space with Aubusson rugs and rose-hued upholstery). All this, while a pianist plays classics on a baby grand. Come hungry!  \n **Good to know** Vegan and gluten-free menus are available with advance notice. Champagne can also be ordered to make your après-midi that much more sparkling. And there’s a pet-friendly tea (complete with a doggie menu), taken outside on the terrace.  \n **Cost** R650 per person (includes a welcome glass of iced tea or L’Ormarins Cap Classique)  \n R350 (children 4 – 11 years)  \n **Times** Monday – Friday, 11am – 4pm (seating slots each hour)  \n Saturday + Sunday, 12pm – 4pm (seating slots each hour)  \n **Contact** 021 483 1000, [[email protected]](/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#562433252237232437382224332533242037223f393825783b383e1634333a3b3938327835393b)  \n **Where to find it** 76 Orange Street, Gardens, Cape Town  \n [Book through DinePlan](https://account.dineplan.com/widgetframe/Fp2gQvbc?partner=10)\n\n## One&Only Cape Town\n\nLofty proportions, grand decor and a magnificent view of Table Mountain through floor-to-ceiling windows greet you as you enter the Vista Bar & Lounge at this elegant V&A Waterfront resort. Served on characterful new V for Vista stands, the [glamorous afternoon tea](https://insideguide.co.za/cape-town/things-to-do/one-and-only-afternoon-tea/) features smoked salmon tartare, Bo Kaap chicken rotis, roast beef sandwiches, tomato and bocconcini blini and more. After the traditional buttermilk scones with cream and jam, move on to the sweet selection: salted caramel taco, mango and passion fruit namelaka, lemon thyme and raspberry choux buns and several more delights. There’s a cake trolley too, and, of course, a free-flowing selection of loose-leaf luxury teas (try two or more throughout the course of your afternoon), or coffee. Or push the boat right out with the elite Champagne Afternoon Tea for two.  \n **Good to know** Vegetarian, vegan and gluten-free menus are also available. Children under 10 can order from the standard Vista Lounge à la carte menu, which includes scones, cakes, pastries, muffins and cookies.  \n **Cost** R650 per person  \n **Times** Monday – Sunday, 2pm – 5pm  \n **Contact** 021 431 5888, [[email protected]](/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#b7d0c2d2c4c3d2cfc7d2c5ded2d9d4d2f7d8d9d2d6d9d3d8d9dbced4d6c7d2c3d8c0d999d4d8da)  \n **Where to find it** Dock Road, V&A Waterfront, Cape Town  \n [Book through DinePlan](https://www.dineplan.com/widgetframe/YkNXr6JD?partner=10)\n\n## The Silo Hotel\n\nTowering above the V&A Waterfront with the most incredible views, The Silo Hotel’s Royal Tea is taken in The Granary Café, a glam sixth-floor space where copper-topped tables and contemporary art complement the striking pillowed-glass windows. Come in your most comfy, loose-waisted clothing because pastry chef Bianca Schreuder puts on quite a spread. Three-tiered circular stands (or *étagères*, to use the correct term) are piled with petit gateaux and exquisite teatime treats: macarons, profiteroles, madeleines and petit fours. The equally delicious savoury stand features delicate finger sandwiches, cheese puffs, mini pies, wraps and quiches, with beautifully balanced flavours and miniature perfection in every bite. And there are scones too. A wide selection of fragrant teas is served in tea-tasting format so that you can try several. We particularly enjoyed the African honeybush.  \n **Good to know** Booking is 24 hours in advance (payment required on booking), or 48 hours for special-occasion, large-group and dietary requests.  \n **Cost** R575 per person  \n R650 (vegetarian, vegan, gluten-free or lactose-free)  \n **Times** Thursday – Sunday, 2.30pm – 5pm (last seating 3.30pm)  \n **Contact** 021 670 0511, [[email protected]](/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#d4b0bdbabdbab394a0bcb1a7bdb8bbbcbba0b1b8fab7bbb9)  \n **Where to find it** Silo Square, S Arm Road, V&A Waterfront, Cape Town  \n [Book through DinePlan](https://account.dineplan.com/widgetframe/gBM9Q637?partner=10)\n\n## The Cellars-Hohenort\n\nThis gracious and historic country hotel in the Constantia Valley is arguably one of the loveliest spots to enjoy high tea at any time of the year. The Classic High Tea is served in the light and airy Greenhouse, or on the Hohenort Terrace with views over the award-winning gardens, which really are rather extraordinary. Impeccably presented and served over four courses with a celebratory glass of bubbly to accompany it, tea meanders from traditional finger sandwiches to savouries, including a delicious Cape Malay-spiced chicken tartlet, and the savoury smoked salmon rillette vol-au-vent. After light and fluffy scones with jam and chantilly cream, move on to nibble on an array of sweet delights: Earl Grey and lavender macarons, chocolate and naartjie opera cake, lemon curd tartlets and more. To drink, there’s a fine selection of 11 loose-leaf Nigiro teas, rooibos and herbal infusions – the exclusive hotel blend of green rooibos with various fruits, flowers, herbs and Liz McGrath rose petals truly conjures a sense of place.  \n **Good to know** Book 24 hours in advance.  \n **Cost** R525 per person  \n R265 (children aged 12 years and under)  \n **Times** Saturday + Sunday, 11am – 1pm +  2pm – 4pm  \n **Contact** 021 794 2137, [[email protected]](/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#6204170c01160b0d0c112201070e0e0310114f0a0d0a070c0d10164c010d4c1803)  \n **Where to find it** 93 Brommersvlei Road, Constantia, Cape Town  \n [Book through DinePlan](https://account.dineplan.com/widgetframe/gKd8sw6P?partner=10)\n\n## Vergenoegd Löw\n\nThe historic Homestead at [Vergenoegd Löw](https://insideguide.co.za/cape-town/vergenoegd-low/) enfolds you in a sense of age-old calm as you settle in for the generous farm tea. This is afternoon tea rooted in the rhythms of the land, harnessing indigenous flavours, seasonal ingredients (many from the farm itself) and the warm traditions of hospitality that infuse every South African farmhouse. Speciality teas from NOOA (Naturally Out of Africa) and creative cold tea “cocktails” accompany a four-course menu which leads with a selection of simply delicious savoury bites: goose terrine, smoked snoek pté infused with indigenous herbs, and a savoury baked cheesecake with a beetroot relish. Generous open sandwiches star homebaked mosbolletjie breads and seed loaves, topped with Coronation chicken, cucumber and amasi, or slices of rare Dexter beef with pomegranate. An interlude for a scone, with grape jam, Dalewood cheese and chantilly cream, segues into the final sweet stand, where the lightest of millefeuille milk tart, a zesty lemon curd meringue tart, a raspberry doughnut and the deepest darkest *moerkoffie* chocolate cake vie for attention. And there’s even the gift of a carrot cake slice to take home.**Good to know** Bookings essential  \n **Cost** R650 per person  \n **Times** Monday – Sunday, 12pm + 2pm (seatings)  \n **Contact** 021 843 3248, [[email protected]](/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#dba9bea8bea9adbaafb2b4b5a89badbea9bcbeb5b4bebcbff5b8b4f5a1ba)  \n **Where to find it** 1 Faure Village Road (off Baden Powell Drive), Somerset West  \n [Book through DinePlan](https://www.dineplan.com/widgetframe/qZPMBgQS?partner=10)\n\n## Orangerie at Le Lude\n\nWhere better to go for a sparkling morning or afternoon tea than an estate that focuses solely on producing award-winning cap classique? The restaurant’s interior hints at a greenhouse of yore, with cane chairs, ceiling fans and leafy palms and ferns. Double-volume arched windows all around put the marvellous view centre stage. Tea begins with a glass of Le Lude Cap Classique (either Brut Reserve or Brut Rosé Reserve), then select two from among six TWG teas. It’s served in the most beautiful tea service, while the eats arrive on a three-tiered platter. The delightful spread starts with finger sandwiches (including Franschhoek smoked trout and caper cream cheese) and savouries such as spinach and gruyere frittata. Then scones with chantilly cream and homemade strawberry jam, followed by exquisite confectionery, from salted caramel profiterole and raspberry macarons to triple chocolate lamington and lemonade madeleines.  \n **Good to know** Book 24 hours in advance. Bookings are for a minimum of two people and maximum of eight guests.  \n **Cost** R590 per person (includes a glass of cap classique)  \n **Times** Monday – Saturday, 10am, 10.30am, 11am, 3pm (seatings)  \n Sunday, 10am, 10.30am, 11am  \n **Contact** 021 876 2961, [[email protected]](/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#deacbbadaabfabacbfb0aa9eb2bbb2abbabbf0bdb1f0a4bf)  \n **Where to find it** Bowling Green Avenue, off Lambrechts Road, Franschhoek  \n [Book through DinePlan](https://www.dineplan.com/widgetframe/wrx2vMGt?partner=10)\n\n## Manor Kitchen at Lanzerac\n\nAdorned with a dazzling chandelier and large skylights, [Lanzerac](https://insideguide.co.za/cape-town/lanzerac/)’s elegant [Manor Kitchen restaurant](https://insideguide.co.za/cape-town/restaurants/manor-kitchen-lanzerac/) is the splendid setting for the Timeless Afternoon Tea. It’s a decidedly decadent and sophisticated affair every Saturday, running from canapés to hot bites to scones, sweet treats and a macaron to finish. After being welcomed with a glass of cap classique, choose from a selection of TWG teas: one tea to pair with your savoury, and a second with the sweet courses. Think reimagined bites prepared with the finest seasonal ingredients, tasty croquettes, a selection of finger sandwiches, as well as Lanzerac’s famous scones served with clotted cream, strawberry jam, lavender cream, cheddar and rich butter. Did we mention the sweets? Cupcakes, petit fours, tartlets, éclairs and a macaroon to finish… You’ll want for nothing except perhaps a loose-fitting frock!  \n **Good to know** There’s also a Gentleman’s Tea in the cosy Craven Lounge on Saturdays that includes a whisky-tasting paired with savoury and sweet bites.  \n **Cost** Timeless Afternoon Tea: R420 per person; R520 with unlimited cap classique  \n Gentleman’s Tea: R490 per person  \n **Times** Saturday, 2pm – 5pm  \n **Contact** 021 887 1132, [[email protected]](/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#3240574146534740535c4641725e535c48574053511c515d1c4853)  \n **Where to find it** Lanzerac Road, Stellenbosch  \n [Book through DinePlan](https://account.dineplan.com/widgetframe/nJ2pW0bN?partner=10)\n\n## La Residence\n\n“Versailles in Franschhoek”, La Residence is an elegant hideaway dramatically staged among sweeping vineyards, orchards and olive groves on a secluded 12-hectare estate. Afternoon Tea is served in beautiful tea sets on The Loggia overlooking a tranquil pond and mountain views, or fireside in The Great Hall on cooler days​. Tiered stands display a variety of savouries, such as mini basil pesto quiches, finger sandwiches, roast beef buns and savoury cheese madeleines, complemented by sweet delights, ranging from classic buttermilk scones (with clotted cream, jam and cheese) to macarons, profiteroles, fynbos honey Swiss roll, passion fruit verrine and chocolate petits fours, and served with Nigiro loose-leaf tea or artisan coffee.  \n **Good to know** Afternoon Tea is for a minimum of two guests.  \n **Cost** R595 per person  \n **Times** Monday – Sunday, 2pm – 5pm  \n **Contact** 021 876 4100, [[email protected]](/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#9df4f3fbf2ddf1fceff8eef4f9f8f3fef8b3fef2b3e7fc)  \n **Where to find it** Off Elandskloof Road, Franschhoek  \n [Enquire Now](https://insideguide.co.za/cape-town/restaurant-enquiry/?activity_provider=info@laresidence.co.za&post_title=High Tea&activity_provider_name=La Residence&booking_for=La Residence High Tea)\n\n## InterContinental Table Bay Cape Town\n\nComfortably settled at a table in the conservatory section of [Flint and Fennel](https://insideguide.co.za/cape-town/restaurants/flint-and-fennel/), with Table Mountain views that anchor you at the tip of the African continent, the afternoon tea is layered with stories and flavours that immerse you in the cultures of this rainbow nation. After a welcome glass of bubbly (or a grapefruit and tea mocktail), classic scones, jam and cream start the ritual, and you choose a different TWG loose-leaf tea for each chapter. Delicious savouries include Franschhoek smoked trout and dill cream cheese roulade, nostalgic mosbolletjies topped with Black Forest ham and caramelised onion, a Coronation chicken pie, and a truffled asparagus quiche. Then linger over a rainbow Unity Cake, a playfully delicious peppermint crisp entremet, milk tart choux buns, a divinely pretty passionfruit-and-frangipane tart, before sipping the final digestif, a Pu-Erh of the Night matured tea with soft notes of star anise.  \n **Cost** R595 per person (including glass of cap classique)  \n **Times** Wednesday – Sunday, 2pm – 4pm  \n **Contact** 021 406 5000  \n **Where to find it** Quay 6, Breakwater Boulevard, V&A Waterfront, Cape Town  \n [Book Now](https://www.opentable.co.uk/booking/restref/availability?lang=en-GB&correlationId=b68ae965-f06f-4d46-8855-f9a9e370c163&restRef=455916&otSource=Restaurant%20website)\n\n## Cape Grace\n\nGlamorous yachts, sunshine sparkling off the water of the marina, with Table Mountain framing it all… The views from [Cape Grace at the V&A Waterfront](https://insideguide.co.za/cape-town/cape-grace/) are pretty special. But once you settle into a comfortable booth at [Heirloom](https://insideguide.co.za/cape-town/restaurants/cape-grace-heirloom/) (the signature restaurant) and embark on [their elegant afternoon tea](https://insideguide.co.za/cape-town/things-to-do/cape-grace-afternoon-tea/), all eyes are on the table. Inspired by his childhood memories of spring in the Karoo, pastry chef Willem Verwey takes you on a journey through the Cape in springtime. Start with canapés of crayfish and buchu, springbok and beetroot, and goats’ cheese and asparagus. Finger sandwiches on the freshest homemade breads continue the theme, with Coronation chicken, grass-fed beef, and classic egg mayo.… followed by the lightest buttermilk scones hot from the oven (and savoury cheese ones too). As the *pièce de resistance*, four exquisite confections express mountain, sunlight, bloom and meadow: a Cape honey sponge with peach and rooibos; passionfruit sponge with banana, lime and guava; a salted crunchy base with dark chocolate mousse, apricot and ginger; and a strawberry and elderflower concoction with lemon verbena, raspberries and chocolate. Accompanied by a selection of beautiful loose-leaf Nigiro tea blends (choose one for the savouries and another for the sweets, or there’s coffee if you prefer), it’s a celebratory spoil to linger over all afternoon.  \n **Good to know** Booking is essential. There is a vegetarian option too.  \n **Cost** R650 per person (includes glass of cap classique or a Cape Grace iced tea)  \n **Times** Thursday – Sunday, 1.30pm, 2pm, 2.30pm, 3pm (seatings)  \n **Contact** 021 410 7100, [[email protected]](/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#9dfefcedf8faeffcfef8b3f5f8f4eff1f2f2f0ddfbfcf4eff0f2f3e9b3fef2f0)  \n **Where to find it** West Quay Road, V&A Waterfront, Cape Town  \n [Book through DinePlan](https://account.dineplan.com/widgetframe/Zfqcz0Qp?partner=10)\n\n## The Vineyard Hotel\n\n[This historic hotel](https://insideguide.co.za/cape-town/accommodation/the-vineyard-hotel/) on the banks of the Liesbeek River is an eight-hectare oasis of verdant gardens, old-world charm and tranquillity. Take [afternoon tea](https://insideguide.co.za/cape-town/things-to-do/vineyard-hotel-afternoon-tea/) – as former owner Lady Anne Barnard might have – on the terrace of Morii restaurant, looking out over the natural splendour and the eastern slopes of Table Mountain. An elegant selection of Italian-inspired savouries, tramezzini, tartlets and warm bites is followed by a delicate array of sweet treats from tiramisu verrines and amaretti cookies to cannoli and scones. And there’s a harvest table stacked with an assortment of classic Italian cakes to enjoy by the slice. Bottomless bubbly or milkshakes and a choice of two teas and/or coffees from the extensive beverage menu completes the experience.  \n **Cost** R525 per person, R250 (children aged 12 and under)  \n R565 (vegan)  \n **Times** Friday – Sunday, 2pm – 4.30pm  \n **Contact** 021 657 4500, [[email protected]](/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#5336322713253a3d362a3221377d303c7d2932)  \n **Where to find it** Colinton Road, Newlands, Cape Town  \n [Book through DinePlan](https://account.dineplan.com/widgetframe/m1TzhNZf?partner=10)\n\n## Erinvale Estate Hotel & Spa\n\nTucked into a leafy garden enclave in a tranquil corner of the Helderberg (adjoining Vergelegen, Lourensford and Morgenster wine estates), [Erinvale Estate Hotel & Spa](https://insideguide.co.za/cape-town/erinvale-estate-hotel/) lay on a sumptuously refined afternoon tea to enjoy in either the chic Magnolia Social Dining Lounge, under the trees at the Terrace, or by the fireplace in the Bistro. Indulge in a beautifully curated menu inspired by local flavours – from savoury favourites like smoked trout roulades, BLT oopsies and chicken liver parfait, to freshly baked mini scones served with cream, cheese and preserves. Sweet endings include rooibos and berry cheesecake, and peppermint crisp macarons – nostalgic classics, lovingly reimagined for a celebratory sense of occasion. Elevate your experience with a glass (or two) of bubbly from their curated selection.  \n **Good to know** Book 48 hours in advance.  \n **Cost** R385 per person  \n **Times** Monday – Sunday, from 12.30pm  \n **Contact** 021 847 1160, [[email protected]](/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#f587908681948087949b8198949b94929087b590879c9b83949990db969adb8f94)  \n **Where to find it** 1 Erinvale Avenue, Somerset West  \n [Enquire Now](https://insideguide.co.za/cape-town/restaurant-enquiry/?activity_provider=restaurantmanager@erinvale.co.za&post_title=High Tea&activity_provider_name=Erinvale&booking_for=Erinvale High Tea)\n\n## Tintswalo Summer House\n\nFor an easy-breezy afternoon tea beside the sea, with a dash of delightful decor and sparkling views of False Bay, this new gem of the Tintswalo Collection fits the brief… Tintswalo Summer House is the former Glencairn Hotel (built in 1904), newly restored and pretty in pink. High tea is taken in the Eatery or, weather permitting, on the terrace or in the small garden. Terrazzo tables are laid with vintage crockery and the tea treats arrive on golden tiered stands. Savoury bites include prawn-and-avo pastry puffs, Cape Malay bobotie spring rolls and Coronation chicken pies, along with four classic finger sandwiches. There are scones, of course, and the sweet delights of cakes (red velvet, carrot, chocolate and Biscoff), macarons and tartlets. Tea sommeliers Clement Moyo or Junias Manomano guide you through the choice of teas and the tales behind them – 12 Nigiro blends inspired by the hotel, its setting and the women behind it. “Lady of the House” is a fruity infusion dedicated to resident ghost Renee; “Cam in Colour” is a flowering tea in honour of the hotel’s interior designer; “Tea with Lisa” is the owner’s favourite tropical-infused green tea; the smoky “Founders’ Gift” is a perfect balance to the sweet eats. There’s also the delicate “Seaside Garden” (lemongrass, chamomile and apple) and refreshing “South-Easterly Wind” (mint, moringa, buchu and lemongrass), and a fynbos blend inspired by a journey along the Garden Route to Glencairn.  \n **Good to know** Booking essential with 24 hours’ notice. Vegetarian/vegan and other dietary restrictions are catered for. Coffee, hot chocolate, mocktails and cocktails are also an option.  \n **Cost** R495 per person (includes a glass of bubbles)  \n **Times** Friday + Saturday, 12pm – 4pm  \n **Contact** 021 773 0220, [[email protected]](/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#542721393931263c3b2127312631373124203d3b3a14203d3a20272335383b7a373b39)  \n **Where to find it** 14 Glen Road, Glencairn, Cape Town  \n [Book through DinePlan](https://account.dineplan.com/widgetframe/6HFLMrhz?partner=10)\n\n## Labotessa X Fragrance Tea\n\nGoing beyond even the palate-tingling decadence of the traditional afternoon tea, this multi-sensory extravaganza takes you on a journey of scent as well as flavour. Starting with a glass of bubbly in the Fragrance Boutique inside Labotessa Boutique Hotel, you embark on a guided tour of the fragrances of the exclusive Parisian parfumerie Diptyque. Then stroll out on to the café & terrace (a leafy haven on Church Square) where a feast of finger sandwiches, scones with rose and strawberry jam, dark chocolate brownies, lemon cake, artisanal macarons and more (each bite inspired by individual fragrance profiles) is paired with floral, woody, citrussy or spicy scents, and with TWG teas. And you get a luxurious Diptyque gift bag to take home with you at the end.  \n **Good to know** Booking is essential. Minimum two guests; only six guests per seating.  \n **Cost** R850 per person  \n **Times** Monday – Sunday, 11am or 2pm  \n **Contact** 021 010 6600, [[email protected]](/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#c1b2b5a0b881ada0a3aeb5a4b2b2a0efa2aeac)  \n **Where to find it** Labotessa Boutique Hotel, 5 Church Square, Cape Town  \n [Enquire Now](https://insideguide.co.za/cape-town/restaurant-enquiry/?activity_provider=stay@labotessa.com&post_title=High Tea&activity_provider_name=Labotessa&booking_for=Labotessa High Tea)\n\n## Grande Roche\n\nThe beautiful thatched Cape Dutch manor at Grande Roche, with its wide shady verandah giving panoramic views over the Paarl mountains, is the perfect setting for an old-world morning or afternoon tea. Arrive to a sparkling glass of cap classique, then settle down to an abundant feast of savouries from beef croquettes and caramelised onion and gruyere tartlets to smoked salmon blini. Generous sweet treats include lemon meringue tartlets, Basque cheesecake, mascarpone and lemon zest cannoli, and chocolate delice accompanied by an enticing menu of whole-leaf and single-estate teas (or coffee if that’s your preferred cuppa).  \n **Good to know** Book 48 hours in advance.  \n **Cost** R595 per person  \n R695 (vegan or gluten-free menu)  \n **Times** Monday – Sunday, 10am – 3.30pm  \n **Contact** 021 863 5100, [[email protected]](/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#f89d8e9d968c8bb89f8a99969c9d8a979b909dd69b97d68299)  \n **Where to find it** 1 Plantasie Street, Paarl  \n [Book through DinePlan](https://account.dineplan.com/widgetframe/PrkDtbT9?partner=10)\n\n## Chocolat Bistro\n\nYou had us at “chocolat”… This French Baroque-inspired eatery in the heart of Durbanville – adorned with vintage pieces, gilt-framed paintings, chandeliers and velvet chairs – is known for its decadent selection of cakes and pastries. With its high tea offering you get the full works… savoury bites include spring rolls with Thai sauce; salami flowers with caper berries; mini quiches; Caprese skewers; salmon blinis; ribeye with truffle mayo on crostini; and mini hamburgers. Then explore the dazzling array of sweet treats: lemon meringue tartlets, canelés, macarons, cheesecakes, pavlovas, chocolate eclairs and chocolate brownies. Outside there’s a gorgeous garden (with children’s play area) and a sun-kissed patio under shady trees.  \n **Good to know** Book three days in advance (50% deposit required). If you’re booking tea for a larger group enquire about The Birdcage, their private venue  \n **Cost** R395 per person; R425 with glass of Pongracz MCC  \n **Times** Monday – Saturday, 9am – 5pm  \n Sunday, 9am – 2pm  \n **Contact** 021 975 2426, [[email protected]](/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#adcfc2c2c6c4c3cadeedcec5c2cec2c1ccd9cfc4ded9dfc283cec283d7cc)  \n **Where to find it** 46 Oxford Street, Durbanville, Cape Town  \n [Enquire Now](https://insideguide.co.za/cape-town/restaurant-enquiry/?activity_provider=bookings@chocolatbistro.co.za&post_title=High Tea&activity_provider_name=Chocolat Bistro&booking_for=Chocolat Bistro High Tea)\n\n## Ladurée\n\nThe *haute* everything of the patisserie world… Paris icon Ladurée is renowned for the most sublime macarons in the world. Their V&A Waterfront tearoom upstairs in the mall (among the world-renowned designer boutique) is beautifully decorated in a swath of pastels and gold, and makes a fitting finale to a retail therapy session with friends. Their afternoon tea for two presents you with tempting choices: a Wagyu croque monsieur, a beef slider or a club sandwich (choose two); two croissants from a selection of plain, chocolate, pistachio or nut; the iconic raspberry ispahan, a traditional tarte citron meringuée, vanilla flan or a glorious plaisir sucrée; and of course two macarons with a rainbow of enticing flavours to deliberate between. Sip a rich dark hot chocolate with cream, excellent coffee or an elegant tea, and sit back and savour an oh-so-chic extravaganza of patisserie spoils.  \n **Good to know** Park at the Woolies end of the underground parking.  \n **Cost** R750 for two (with tea, coffee or hot chocolate)  \n R975 for two (with a glass of cap classique each)  \n **Times** Monday – Sunday, 12pm – 5pm  \n **Contact** 021 141 2987  \n **Where to find it** Upper Level, Victoria Wharf Shopping Centre, V&A Waterfront, Cape Town  \n [Book through DinePlan](https://www.dineplan.com/widgetframe/d5YflhCQ?partner=10)\n\n## Hazendal\n\nRelaxed elegance is the default setting at Marks, a comfortable dining lounge set between the tasting room and the working cellars at Hazendal. On weekend afternoons (Fridays too) live piano music sets the scene for a gracious afternoon tea. A leisurely menu takes you from spekboom and cucumber gazpacho to pulled lamb with waterblommetjie pickle, followed by lemon scones with citrus butter. Delicate sweet creations featuring lemon verbena, thyme and gooseberry conclude the feast. A choice of speciality teas accompany the delectable ritual from peach jasmine oolong and darjeeling first flush to Cederberg rooibos and herbal infusions. A floral cocktail is also available as an optional addition on arrival.  \n **Cost** R450 per person  \n **Times** Friday – Sunday, 2.30pm  \n **Contact** 021 903 5034, [[email protected]](/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#d0a2b5a3b5a2a6b1a4b9bfbea390b8b1aab5beb4b1bcfeb3bfbd)  \n **Where to find it** Hazendal Wine Estate, Bottelary Road, Stellenbosch Winelands  \n [Book       "title": "Cape Town - Wikipedia",
      "content": "The city has a rich history, and several notable cultural attractions. Within Cape Town CBD there are the Artscape and District Six Homecoming Center theaters, The Houses of Parliament, the Iziko museums and Planetarium, the South African National Gallery, the Castle of Good Hope, and The Company's Garden (South Africa's oldest park). The CBD is also home to many coffeehouses, as well as numerous restaurants that provide a variety of local cuisine options.\n\nThe Victoria & Alfred Waterfront is a mixed-use development built on top of part of the docks \"Dock (maritime)\") of the Port of Cape Town, and is the city's most-visited tourist attraction. It is also one of the city's most popular shopping venues, with several hundred shops as well as the Two Oceans Aquarium. [...] The city is known for its harbour, its natural setting in the Cape Floristic Region, and for landmarks such as Table Mountain and Cape Point. Located on the shore of Table Bay, the City Bowl area of Cape Town, which contains its central business district (CBD), is the oldest urban area in the Western Cape, with a significant cultural heritage. The metropolitan area has a long coastline on the Atlantic Ocean, which includes a northern section in the West Beach region, as well as the False Bay area in the south. [...] The city has gained international recognition for its pizza scene. In 2025, then-new, family-owned restaurant NOVO, based in Mowbray, ranked in publication Time Out's Top 20 Pizzas globally. It did so for its \"The Fellini\" creation. In the 2026 ranking from the same publication, Woodstock-based establishment Pizza Connection ranked 11th on the list, for its \"The Capetonian\" pizza.\n\nCoffee culture in Cape Town is thriving, with many independent coffeehouses and cafe chains with locations across the city. All four of South Africa's largest cafe chains, Vida e Caffè, Seattle Coffee Company, WCafe \"Woolworths (South Africa)\"), and Bootlegger Coffee Company, are headquartered in Cape Town. Furthermore, the city is home to 10 roasteries.",
