|\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/Cape_Town_population_pyramid.svg/250px-Cape_Town_population_pyramid.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Cape_Town_population_pyramid.svg)\n\nCape Town population pyramid in 2011\n\n [![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2d/Cape_Town_2011_population_density_map.svg/250px-Cape_Town_2011_population_density_map.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Cape_Town_population_density_map.svg)\n\nPopulation density in Cape Town\n\nAccording to the [South African National Census of 2011](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_National_Census_of_2011 \"South African National Census of 2011\"), the population of the City of Cape Town metropolitan municipality – an area that includes suburbs and [exurbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exurb \"Exurb\") – is 3,740,026 people. This represents an annual growth rate of 2.6% compared to the results of the [previous census in 2001](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_National_Census_of_2001 \"South African National Census of 2001\") which found a population of 2,892,243 people.[[135]](#cite_note-wc-muni-report-137) : 54\n\nOf those residents who were asked about their [first language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_language \"First language\"), 35.7% spoke [Afrikaans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afrikaans \"Afrikaans\"), 29.8% spoke [Xhosa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xhosa_language \"Xhosa language\") and 28.4% spoke [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_English \"South African English\"). 24.8% of the population is under the age of 15, while 5.5% is 65 or older.[[135]](#cite_note-wc-muni-report-137): 64 The [sex ratio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_sex_ratio \"Human sex ratio\") is 0.96, meaning that there are slightly more women than men.[[135]](#cite_note-wc-muni-report-137): 55\n\nOf those residents aged 20 or older, 1.8% have no schooling, 8.1% have some schooling but did not finish primary school, 4.6% finished primary school but have no secondary schooling, 38.9% have some secondary schooling but did not finish [Grade 12](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grade_12 \"Grade 12\"), 29.9% finished Grade 12 but have no higher education, and 16.7% have higher education.\n\nOverall, 46.6% have at least a Grade 12 education.[[135]](#cite_note-wc-muni-report-137): 74 Of those aged between 5 and 25, 67.8% are attending an educational institution.[[135]](#cite_note-wc-muni-report-137): 78 Amongst those aged between 15 and 65 the unemployment rate is 23.7%.[[135]](#cite_note-wc-muni-report-137): 79\n\nThe average annual household income in the [Western Cape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Cape \"Western Cape\"), of which Cape Town is the capital and largest city, is South Africa's highest, by a large margin. As of June 2025, the province's average household income is [R](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_rand \"South African rand\")407,000. This amount is 35% higher than second-place [Gauteng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gauteng \"Gauteng\"), and 50% above the national average.[[20]](#cite_note-South_Africans_flock_to_Cape_Town-22)\n\nThe total number of households grew from 653,085 in 1996 to 1,068,572 in 2011, which represents an increase of 63.6%.[[135]](#cite_note-wc-muni-report-137): 81 The average number of household members declined from 3,92 in 1996 to 3,50 in 2011.[[136]](#cite_note-Cape_Town_2016-138) Of those households, 78.4% are in formal structures (houses or [flats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apartment \"Apartment\")), while 20.5% are in informal structures ([shacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shanty_town \"Shanty town\")).[[135]](#cite_note-wc-muni-report-137): 81\n\n97.3% of City-supplied households have access to electricity,[[137]](#cite_note-139) and 94.0% of households use [electricity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mains_electricity \"Mains electricity\") for lighting.[[135]](#cite_note-wc-muni-report-137): 84 87.3% of households have [piped water](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_supply \"Water supply\") to the dwelling, while 12.0% have piped water through a communal tap.[[135]](#cite_note-wc-muni-report-137): 85 94.9% of households have regular [refuse collection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Refuse_collection \"Refuse collection\") service.[[135]](#cite_note-wc-muni-report-137): 86\n\n91.4% of households have a [flush toilet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flush_toilet \"Flush toilet\") or [chemical toilet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chemical_toilet \"Chemical toilet\"), while 4.5% still use a bucket toilet.[[135]](#cite_note-wc-muni-report-137): 87 82.1% of households have a [refrigerator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Refrigerator \"Refrigerator\"), 87.3% have a television and 70.1% have a radio. Only 34.0% have a landline telephone, but 91.3% have a cellphone. 37.9% have a computer, and 49.3% have access to the Internet (either through a computer or a cellphone).[[135]](#cite_note-wc-muni-report-137)\n\nIn 2011 over 70% of cross provincial South African migrants coming into the Western Cape settled in Cape Town; 53.64% of South African migrants into the Western Cape came from the [Eastern Cape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Cape \"Eastern Cape\"), the old Cape Colony's former native reserve, and 20.95% came from Gauteng province.[[138]](#cite_note-140)\n\nCape Town has a young population. In the 2022 [census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Census \"Census\"), the median age of residents in the city was 31. In the same year, approximately 40% of the population of Cape Town was under the age of 25.[[139]](#cite_note-2022_Census_CCT_Profile-141)\n\nAccording to the 2016 City of Cape Town community survey, there were 4,004,793 people in the City of Cape Town metro. Out of this population, 45.7% identified as Black African, 35.1% identified as Coloured, 16.2% identified as White and 1.6% identified as Asian.[[140]](#cite_note-142)\n\nThe Western Cape province, in which Cape Town is the largest city, has the highest rate of marriage out of all South African provinces, by a significant margin. In the 2022 census, 33.8% of those in the province were legally married – almost 10% higher than the next province. In the same census, 50.6% of Western Cape residents had never been married – the lowest out of all South African provinces.[[141]](#cite_note-Census_2022_-_Statistical_Release-143)\n\nDuring the outbreak of the [COVID-19 pandemic in South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_South_Africa \"COVID-19 pandemic in South Africa\"), local media reported that increasing numbers of wealthy and middle-class South Africans have started moving from inland areas of the country to coastal regions – most notably Cape Town – in a phenomenon referred to as \"*semigration*\" – short for \"semi-emigration\"[[142]](#cite_note-Staff_Writer-144)[[143]](#cite_note-145)[[144]](#cite_note-146) Declining municipal services in the rest of the country and the [South African energy crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_energy_crisis \"South African energy crisis\") are other cited reasons for semigration.[[145]](#cite_note-147)\n\nIn 2022, the city expected its population to grow by an additional 400,000 residents between 2020 and 2025, with 76% of those new residents falling into the low-income bracket, earning less than [R](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_rand \"South African rand\")13,000 a month.[[146]](#cite_note-148)\n\nAccording to the United Nations' World Urbanization Prospects (2018), Cape Town's population is projected to continue growing steadily over the next decade. Based on estimates compiled by the Department of Economic and Social Affairs in 2018, the city's population is expected to reach approximately 5.468 million by 2030, and roughly 5.845 million by 2035.[[147]](#cite_note-UNWUP2018-149)\n\nEthnic make-up of Cape Town\n\n| Race | 1996 | 2001 | 2011 | 2022 |\n| Number | Percent | Number | Percent | Number | Percent | Number | Percent |\n| Black | 644,181 | 25.10% | 916,635 | 31.70% | 1,444,939 | 38.60% | 2,181,190 | 45.70% |\n| Coloured | 1,239,943 | 48.40% | 1,392,594 | 48.10% | 1,585,286 | 42.40% | 1,670,496 | 35.00% |\n| White | 543,425 | 21.20% | 542,540 | 18.80% | 585,831 | 15.70% | 773,201 | 16.20% |\n| Indian/Asian | 37,882 | 1.50% | 41,477 | 1.40% | 43,593 | 1.40% | 76,365 | 1.60% |\n\n[[148]](#cite_note-CCT2016-150)\n\n### Religion\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=22 \"Edit section: Religion\")]\n\nSee also: [Religion in South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_South_Africa \"Religion in South Africa\")\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/26/00000-St_Georges_Cathedral-Cape_Town-s.jpg/250px-00000-St_Georges_Cathedral-Cape_Town-s.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:00000-St_Georges_Cathedral-Cape_Town-s.jpg)\n\n[St George's Anglican Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._George's_Cathedral,_Cape_Town \"St. George's Cathedral, Cape Town\") is one of the largest and oldest religious sites in the city.\n\nIn the [2022 South African Census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_South_African_Census \"2022 South African Census\"), religious data were gathered for the [Western Cape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Cape \"Western Cape\") province, of which Cape Town is the capital and largest city (with 64.21% of the province's population residing in the [City of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Cape_Town \"City of Cape Town\") metro area). The Western Cape census respondents self-identified as follows:[[149]](#cite_note-Census_2022_Statistical_Release-151)\n\n* 85.6% as [Christian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christians \"Christians\")\n* 5.2% as [Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslims \"Muslims\")\n* 5.3% as following a [traditional African religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traditional_African_religions \"Traditional African religions\")\n* 2% as having [no religious affiliation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligious \"Irreligious\")\n* 0.8% as \"other\"\n* 0.3% as [Agnostic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agnostic \"Agnostic\")\n* 0.3% as [Atheist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atheist \"Atheist\")\n* 0.2% as [Hindu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu \"Hindu\")\n* 0.2% as [Jewish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish \"Jewish\")\n* 0.1% as [Buddhist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhist \"Buddhist\")\n\nAs of 2022, a total of 2.6% of Western Cape residents are [irreligious](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligious \"Irreligious\").[[149]](#cite_note-Census_2022_Statistical_Release-151)\n\nMost [places of worship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Places_of_worship \"Places of worship\") in Cape Town are Christian churches and cathedrals. These include the [Dutch Reformed Church in South Africa (NGK)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_Reformed_Church_in_South_Africa_(NGK) \"Dutch Reformed Church in South Africa (NGK)\"), [Zion Christian Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zion_Christian_Church \"Zion Christian Church\"), [Apostolic Faith Mission of South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apostolic_Faith_Mission_of_South_Africa \"Apostolic Faith Mission of South Africa\"), [Assemblies of God](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assemblies_of_God \"Assemblies of God\"), [Baptist Union of Southern Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baptist_Union_of_Southern_Africa \"Baptist Union of Southern Africa\") (Baptist World Alliance), [Methodist Church of Southern Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Methodist_Church_of_Southern_Africa \"Methodist Church of Southern Africa\") (World Methodist Council), [Anglican Church of Southern Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglican_Church_of_Southern_Africa \"Anglican Church of Southern Africa\") (Anglican Communion), [Presbyterian Church of Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presbyterian_Church_of_Africa \"Presbyterian Church of Africa\") (World Communion of Reformed Churches), [Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Cape_Town \"Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Cape Town\") (Catholic Church),[[150]](#cite_note-152) the [Orthodox Archbishopric of Good Hope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_Orthodox_Patriarchate_of_Alexandria \"Greek Orthodox Patriarchate of Alexandria\") ([Greek Orthodox Cathedral of St George](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_Orthodox_Cathedral_of_St_George \"Greek Orthodox Cathedral of St George\")),[[151]](#cite_note-153) and [the Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Church_of_Jesus_Christ_of_Latter-day_Saints \"The Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints\") (LDS Church).[[152]](#cite_note-154)\n\n[Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam \"Islam\") is the city's second largest religion with a [long history in Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_South_Africa \"Islam in South Africa\").[[153]](#cite_note-155) This has resulted in a number of mosques and other Muslim religious sites spread across the city,[[154]](#cite_note-156) such as the [Auwal Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auwal_Mosque \"Auwal Mosque\") - South Africa's first mosque.\n\nCape Town's significant [Jewish population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_South_Africa \"History of the Jews in South Africa\") supports a number of synagogues, the most notable of which is the historic [Gardens Shul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gardens_Shul \"Gardens Shul\"), the oldest Jewish congregation in South Africa.[[155]](#cite_note-forward-157) [Marais Road Shul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marais_Road_Shul \"Marais Road Shul\"), located in [Sea Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_Point \"Sea Point\") (a suburb with a notable Jewish population), is the largest Jewish congregation in South Africa.[[156]](#cite_note-158) [Temple Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_Israel_(Cape_Town) \"Temple Israel (Cape Town)\") (Cape Town Progressive Jewish Congregation) also has three temples in the city.[[157]](#cite_note-159) Other affiliated institutions include a [Chabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chabad \"Chabad\") centre in Sea Point and a [Chabad on Campus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chabad_on_Campus_International_Foundation \"Chabad on Campus International Foundation\") at the [University of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Cape_Town \"University of Cape Town\"), catering to Jewish students.[[158]](#cite_note-160)\n\nOther religious sites in the city include [Hindu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu \"Hindu\") and [Buddhist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhism \"Buddhism\") temples and centres.[[159]](#cite_note-161)[[160]](#cite_note-162)\n\n### Crime\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=23 \"Edit section: Crime\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4f/Smash_and_Grab_Hot_Spot%2C_Retreat_%28South_Africa%29.jpg/250px-Smash_and_Grab_Hot_Spot%2C_Retreat_%28South_Africa%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Smash_and_Grab_Hot_Spot,_Retreat_(South_Africa).jpg)\n\nSign warning motorists about the prevalence of [smash and grab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smash_and_grab \"Smash and grab\") incidents at a \"Crime Hot Spot\" on the [M5 freeway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M5_(Cape_Town) \"M5 (Cape Town)\"), between the lower-income neighbourhoods of [Grassy Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grassy_Park \"Grassy Park\") and [Retreat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Retreat,_Cape_Town \"Retreat, Cape Town\"), situated in the [Cape Flats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Flats \"Cape Flats\") region of the city\n\n#### Overview\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=24 \"Edit section: Overview\")]\n\nIn recent years, the [City of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Cape_Town \"City of Cape Town\") ([metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_municipality_(South_Africa) \"Metropolitan municipality (South Africa)\") government) has made significant progress in combatting crime across Cape Town. Measures include the expanded use of digital tools, such as [CCTV cameras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CCTV_camera \"CCTV camera\") and [UAVs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Use_of_unmanned_aerial_vehicles_in_law_enforcement \"Use of unmanned aerial vehicles in law enforcement\"); increased Highway Patrol presence; substantially increased numbers of Law Enforcement officers; dispatching specialized teams of officers to areas based on the types of localized crimes there; targeting areas of specifically high crime; and lobbying the [national government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_South_Africa \"Government of South Africa\") for more control (via the [devolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Devolution \"Devolution\") of powers) over local policing.[[161]](#cite_note-City_patrols_reshape_safety_picture_on_major_highways-163)\n\nMeasures also include facilitating collaboration between various local and provincial law enforcement agencies and units (including the Traffic Service, Highway Patrol, Flying Squad, Ghost Squad, Law Enforcement, LEAP, LEAS, Disaster Volunteers, Gang and Drug Task Team, Metal Theft Unit, and SSU).[[161]](#cite_note-City_patrols_reshape_safety_picture_on_major_highways-163)\n\nOverall, Cape Town has historically had high levels of violent crime. However, these city-wide statistics are heavily skewed and predominantly driven by [gang violence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gang_violence \"Gang violence\") in specific suburbs, many of which are located in the lower-income [Cape Flats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Flats \"Cape Flats\") region of the city.[[162]](#cite_note-Recorded_Crime_Statistics_Republic_Of_South_Africa:_Second_Quarter_of_2025_to_2026_Financial_Year_(July_2025_to_September_2025)-164) This situation has created quite a disparity between safety levels amongst residents of Cape Town, with those in many of the city's suburbs enjoying a life devoid of the kinds of risks faced by those living in and commuting through high-crime neighbourhoods.[[67]](#cite_note-:3-69)[[163]](#cite_note-:4-165)[[164]](#cite_note-Violent_crime_in_South_Africa_happens_mostly_in_a_few_hotspots:_police_resources_should_focus_there_–_criminologist-166)\n\nCrime in Cape Town in general is attributed to various factors, many of which are shared with other major [South African metros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_municipality_(South_Africa) \"Metropolitan municipality (South Africa)\"). These include [economic inequality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_inequality \"Economic inequality\") (which can be linked to the legacy of [apartheid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apartheid \"Apartheid\")'s spatial and social divisions), [unemployment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unemployment \"Unemployment\"), [alcohol abuse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alcohol_abuse \"Alcohol abuse\"), the prevalence of illegal [firearms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firearms \"Firearms\"), a lack of rehabilitation and support services for offenders leading to [recidivism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recidivism \"Recidivism\"), and a lack of community safety organizations.[[67]](#cite_note-:3-69)[[163]](#cite_note-:4-165)[[164]](#cite_note-Violent_crime_in_South_Africa_happens_mostly_in_a_few_hotspots:_police_resources_should_focus_there_–_criminologist-166)\n\nCrime in Cape Town is a serious problem which affects the [quality of life](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quality_of_life \"Quality of life\") and safety of its residents and visitors. Between 2022 and 2023, Cape Town recorded the highest number of murders in a single year of any city in the world, at 2,998, followed by fellow South African metros [Johannesburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johannesburg \"Johannesburg\") and [Durban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durban \"Durban\"). This represented an increase of 8.6% year-on-year.[[165]](#cite_note-167)[[166]](#cite_note-168) Household crimes including [burglary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burglary \"Burglary\") also increased in the same period.[[167]](#cite_note-169) In 2022, [Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexico \"Mexico\")'s Citizen Council for Public Security and Criminal Justice ranked Cape Town among the most violent cities in the world, based on average crime statistics across all suburbs.[[168]](#cite_note-170)\n\nThe minibus taxi industry has been the source of a number of [violent confrontations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taxi_wars_in_South_Africa \"Taxi wars in South Africa\") in the city. The northern and eastern sections of the city was the scene of the [2021 Cape Town taxi conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Cape_Town_taxi_conflict \"2021 Cape Town taxi conflict\"), a violent turf war which led to 83 deaths. The [2023 Cape Town taxi strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Cape_Town_taxi_strike \"2023 Cape Town taxi strike\") resulted in 5 recorded deaths.[[169]](#cite_note-171)[[170]](#cite_note-172)\n\n#### Localization of crime\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=25 \"Edit section: Localization of crime\")]\n\nAs with other major metropolitan areas in South Africa, the majority of crime in Cape Town tends to be concentrated in very specific areas, more often than not being the Cape Flats, indicating the potential success of place-based crime intelligence methods.[[164]](#cite_note-Violent_crime_in_South_Africa_happens_mostly_in_a_few_hotspots:_police_resources_should_focus_there_–_criminologist-166)\n\nIn Cape Town, a lot of the crime that occurs, especially violent crime, takes place in the neighbourhoods of [Philippi East](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippi_East?action=edit&redlink=1 \"Philippi East (page does not exist)\") (a notorious area for such crime), Delft, Nyanga, Mfuleni, Gugulethu, Elsies River, Bishop Lavis, Grassy Park, Lentegeur, Kleinvlei, and Mitchell's Plain. All of these suburbs are best avoided (even during transit) by locals and tourists.[[171]](#cite_note-Crime_stats:_Murder_down_nearly_10%,_but_an_average_of_75_people_killed_daily-173)[[162]](#cite_note-Recorded_Crime_Statistics_Republic_Of_South_Africa:_Second_Quarter_of_2025_to_2026_Financial_Year_(July_2025_to_September_2025)-164)\n\nOther notable areas for violent crime include Kraaifontein, Atlantis, Athlone, Kleinvlei, Khayelitsha, Harare, Bishop Lavis, Muizenberg, Lingelethu-West, Table View, [Bellville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bellville,_Western_Cape \"Bellville, Western Cape\"), Parow, Goodwood, Milnerton, and Makhaza. These suburbs are also best avoided (even during transit) by locals and tourists.[[171]](#cite_note-Crime_stats:_Murder_down_nearly_10%,_but_an_average_of_75_people_killed_daily-173)[[162]](#cite_note-Recorded_Crime_Statistics_Republic_Of_South_Africa:_Second_Quarter_of_2025_to_2026_Financial_Year_(July_2025_to_September_2025)-164)\n\nNotable areas for high rates of non-violent crime include [Cape Town CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD \"Cape Town CBD\"), Steenberg, Wynberg, Sea Point, Woodstock, Samora Machel, Claremont, Somerset West, Brackenfell, Ravensmead, Philippi, and Strand.[[162]](#cite_note-Recorded_Crime_Statistics_Republic_Of_South_Africa:_Second_Quarter_of_2025_to_2026_Financial_Year_(July_2025_to_September_2025)-164)\n\n#### Travellers\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=26 \"Edit section: Travellers\")]\n\nWhile the [UK Foreign Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign,_Commonwealth_and_Development_Office \"Foreign, Commonwealth and Development Office\") considers Cape Town safe to travel to, it notes high crime rates on certain roads leading to and from [Cape Town International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_International_Airport \"Cape Town International Airport\"). These include the [R300](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R300_(South_Africa) \"R300 (South Africa)\") regional road, and Borcherds Quarry Road. The Foreign Office recommends using the [M3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M3_(Cape_Town) \"M3 (Cape Town)\") metropolitan freeway and [N2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N2_(South_Africa) \"N2 (South Africa)\") national freeway instead of the R300, and recommends using the Airport Approach Road (Exit 16 on the N2), instead of Borcherds Quarry Road. Doing so allows drivers to avoid being routed to and through high-crime areas and intersections.[[172]](#cite_note-Foreign_Travel_Advice_-_South_Africa-174)[[173]](#cite_note-FCDO_updates_safety_advice_for_travellers_to_South_Africa-175)\n\n#### Community initiatives\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=27 \"Edit section: Community initiatives\")]\n\nFor certain activities in which locals partake, members of the community have come together to increase safety in Cape Town. An example for [hiking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hiking \"Hiking\") - a popular pastime in Cape Town - is the Hikers Network's[[174]](#cite_note-The_Hikers_Network_-_Homepage-176) SafetyMountain Tracking, a [WhatsApp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WhatsApp \"WhatsApp\")-based emergency service manned by volunteers. The service tracks hikers and other [mountain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mountain \"Mountain\") users (such as [runners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Running \"Running\") and [cyclists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cycling \"Cycling\")) on their adventures, giving them instant access to assistance in case of an emergency, such as injury, sudden illness, rockfall, getting lost, and crime.[[175]](#cite_note-SafetyMountain_Tracking-177)\n\n#### Recent improvements\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=28 \"Edit section: Recent improvements\")]\n\nIn early 2025, the City of Cape Town announced that progress had been made in combatting crime, with crime levels in certain notorious areas decreasing, according to statistics based on community reporting of incidents of crime. Daily patrol officers were complimented for their efforts, and the City said it was working closely with the [Western Cape Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Cape_Government \"Western Cape Government\") and the [South African Police Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_Police_Service \"South African Police Service\") to adjust its law enforcement efforts according to changes in the city's crime statistics.[[176]](#cite_note-Cape_Town_police_stations_lead_in_SA_murder_statistics-178)\n\nDespite the crime levels, the city's [economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy \"Economy\") has grown, as a result of good governance, investments in infrastructure, and an attractive business environment, as well as due to growth in the local [tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism \"Tourism\") and [real estate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_estate \"Real estate\") industries.[[177]](#cite_note-179) Since July 2019 widespread violent crime in poorer [gang dominated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gangs_in_South_Africa \"Gangs in South Africa\") areas of greater Cape Town has resulted in an ongoing military presence in these neighbourhoods.[[178]](#cite_note-180)[[179]](#cite_note-181)\n\nIn terms of minibus taxi safety, the [Western Cape Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Cape_Government \"Western Cape Government\") now takes to special measures during times of high crime, whereby it temporarily shuts down specific routes that are directly impacted. The Province is able to invoke Section 91 of the National Land Transport Act, a legal provision that allows a provincial government to impose extraordinary measures when violence, unrest, or instability in the public transport sector places lives at risk. Such measures were taken by the Western Cape Mobility Department for 30 days (with a subsequent extension), on routes in Mfuleni, Somerset West, Khayelitsha, Nomzamo, and Lwandle, in late 2025.[[180]](#cite_note-Western_Cape_Mobility_on_closure_of_certain_minibus_taxi_routes_in_response_to_ongoing_violence-182)\n\nIn 2025, Cape Town's Mayoral Committee Member (MCM) for Safety and Security, [JP Smith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/JP_Smith_(politician) \"JP Smith (politician)\"), said that the expanded use of digital tools to complement [law enforcement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_enforcement \"Law enforcement\") efforts has become part of the city's [public safety](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_safety \"Public safety\") operations. These tools include a vast network of [CCTV cameras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CCTV_camera \"CCTV camera\") and [drones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Use_of_unmanned_aerial_vehicles_in_law_enforcement \"Use of unmanned aerial vehicles in law enforcement\"), which have already proven successful in assisting with both arrests and prosecution.[[181]](#cite_note-Large_South_African_city_using_cameras_and_drones_to_stop_crime-183)\n\n## Economy\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=29 \"Edit section: Economy\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6b/Cape_Town_City_DSC_3621_%28cropped%29.jpg/1280px-Cape_Town_City_DSC_3621_%28cropped%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Cape_Town_City_DSC_3621_(cropped).jpg)\n\n[Cape Town CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD \"Cape Town CBD\") is the main economic hub of the city, housing the headquarters of many of South Africa's largest companies, as well as numerous tourist attractions and amenities for residents\n\nSee also: [Economy of the Western Cape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_the_Western_Cape \"Economy of the Western Cape\")\n\n|  |\n| --- 60 | [Equites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Equites_(company) \"Equites (company)\") | [Real estate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_estate \"Real estate\") | R15.31 billion |\n| 62 | [Coronation Fund Managers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coronation_Fund_Managers \"Coronation Fund Managers\") | Finance | R14.93 billion |\n| 63 | [Fairvest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fairvest \"Fairvest\") | Real estate | R14.33 billion |\n| 64 | [Pick n Pay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pick_n_Pay \"Pick n Pay\") | Retail | R13.93 billion |\n| 68 | [Hosken Consolidated Investments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hosken_Consolidated_Investments \"Hosken Consolidated Investments\") | Finance | R11.91 billion |\n| 84 | [Stor-Age Property REIT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stor-Age_Property_REIT \"Stor-Age Property REIT\") | Real estate | R8.2 billion |\n| 85 | Oceana Group | [Food](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_industry \"Food industry\") | R7.75 billion |\n| 103 | [Spear REIT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spear_REIT \"Spear REIT\") | Real estate | R5.27 billion |\n| 105 | [Sygnia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sygnia \"Sygnia\") | Finance | R4.87 billion |\n|\n\nCape Town is [Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Africa \"Africa\")'s third-largest economic hub, and South Africa's second-largest by annual gross metropolitan output. It serves as the regional financial and manufacturing center in the [Western Cape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Cape \"Western Cape\") province.\n\nThe city has a diverse economy that is not significantly reliant on any particular sector. Major industries in Cape Town include tech, renewable energy, retail, finance, boat building, film, and conferences.\n\nThe financial sector accounts for 22% of the Western Cape's annual economic output, as of 2025.\n\nCape Town is considered a global [tech hub](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tech_hub \"Tech hub\"), and is home to numerous startups, startup incubators, and large tech companies.[[183]](#cite_note-Cape_Town:_Africa’s_Tech_Hub_on_the_Rise-185) In 2025, Cape Town's tech sector was growing at an annual rate of 8%.\n\nThe city also has a substantial Green Economy, with numerous [renewable energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renewable_energy \"Renewable energy\") companies headquartered in the metro, and around R15 billion invested into the sector in 2024 alone.[[20]](#cite_note-South_Africans_flock_to_Cape_Town-22)\n\n### Income levels\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=30 \"Edit section: Income levels\")]\n\nAs of June 2025, Cape Town has South Africa's highest average household income, as well as the country's lowest rate of unemployment, and strongest-performing property market.[[24]](#cite_note-CT-26)\n\nThe average annual household income in the [Western Cape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Cape \"Western Cape\") is the highest in South Africa. As of June 2025, the province's average household income is [R](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_rand \"South African rand\")407,000. This amount is 35% higher than second-place [Gauteng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gauteng \"Gauteng\"), and 50% above the national average.[[20]](#cite_note-South_Africans_flock_to_Cape_Town-22)\n\n### Employment levels\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=31 \"Edit section: Employment levels\")]\n\nThe professional, scientific, and technical services sector accounts for 23.34% (around 254,600 people) of the city's employment, which is the highest of any other industry.\n\nThe construction industry is the third-largest by number of employees, as it accounts for 9.29% (around 101,300 people) of the city's employment. The manufacturing sector, the second largest sector, also accounts for a large part of the city's employment opportunities as it provides 18.15% of the city's employment.[[184]](#cite_note-186)\n\nWhereas unemployment has increased in numerous major cities across the country in recent years, in Cape Town, employment has increased. In 2025, it was reported that the city had diverged from a national trend, and added 86,000 jobs. The [Mayor of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayor_of_Cape_Town \"Mayor of Cape Town\") attributed this growth to good governance and [infrastructure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infrastructure \"Infrastructure\") investments, creating an attractive [business](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Business \"Business\") environment, and an increase in [tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism \"Tourism\").[[21]](#cite_note-Cape_Town_Deviates_from_National_Labour_Trends-23)\n\nThe Western Cape's unemployment levels are below the national average. Formal sector jobs in the Western Cape grew by 3.1% in 2024 - 61% higher than the national average.[[20]](#cite_note-South_Africans_flock_to_Cape_Town-22)\n\n### Economic output and growth\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=32 \"Edit section: Economic output and growth\")]\n\nIn 2008, Cape Town was considered the most [entrepreneurial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entrepreneurship \"Entrepreneurship\") city in South Africa, with the percentage of Capetonians pursuing business opportunities almost three times higher than the national average. Capetonians aged between 18 and 64 were 190% more likely to pursue new business, whilst in Johannesburg, the same demographic group was only 60% more likely than the national average to pursue a new business.[[185]](#cite_note-187)\n\nIn 2019, the city's [GMP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gross_metropolitan_product \"Gross metropolitan product\") of R489 billion[[186]](#cite_note-Cape_Town_2020-188) (US$33.04 billion)[[187]](#cite_note-SARS_2019-189) represented 71.1% of the Western Cape's total [GRP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gross_regional_domestic_product \"Gross regional domestic product\") and 9.6% of South Africa's total GDP;[[186]](#cite_note-Cape_Town_2020-188) the city also accounted for 11.1%[[186]](#cite_note-Cape_Town_2020-188) of all employed people in the country and had a citywide [GDP per capita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GDP_per_capita \"GDP per capita\") of R111,364[[186]](#cite_note-Cape_Town_2020-188) (US$7,524).[[187]](#cite_note-SARS_2019-189)\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/19/Construction_Site_in_Tokai%2C_Cape_Town.jpg/330px-Construction_Site_in_Tokai%2C_Cape_Town.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Construction_Site_in_Tokai,_Cape_Town.jpg)\n\nA commercial construction site in the suburb of [Tokai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tokai,_Cape_Town \"Tokai, Cape Town\")\n\nAs of 2024, Cape Town's labor force consists of 1.1 million workers. The largest sector in Cape Town is the professional and business services industry, which accounts for 29.61% of the city's workforce.\n\nCape Town is home to the headquarters of many of South Africa's largest companies, across a variety of industries, many of which are located in its [CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD \"Cape Town CBD\"). As of 2022 data, 8 of South Africa's 50 [largest companies by annual revenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_largest_companies_in_South_Africa \"List of largest companies in South Africa\") were headquartered in Cape Town.\n\nCape Town has significantly above-average economic performance amongst its South African [metropolitan peers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_municipality_(South_Africa) \"Metropolitan municipality (South Africa)\"), and numerous companies have moved employees and their headquarters to the city in recent years.[[188]](#cite_note-Cape_Town_hot_spot_for_building_opportunities,_Gauteng_market_softening_–_WBHO-190) In 2025, according to [Statistics South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Statistics_South_Africa \"Statistics South Africa\") (Stats SA), Cape Town had the lowest unemployment rate out of all South African cities.\n\n### Major industries\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=33 \"Edit section: Major industries\")]\n\nCape Town's economy consists of a diverse mix of industries. The [professional](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Professional_services \"Professional services\"), scientific, and technical services sector forms a major part of the local economy. This sector includes a variety of fields, such as software development, management, accounting, consulting, architecture, design, engineering, legal, and RND.\n\n#### Tech\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=34 \"Edit section: Tech\")]\n\nCape Town is recognized as a global tech hub, and is considered Africa's main tech hub. The city is home to the headquarters of many startups and established tech companies. Growing at an annual rate of 8.5%, the [tech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Technology_company \"Technology company\") industry is a major part of Cape Town's economy.[[189]](#cite_note-IT_leads-191) Cape Town is home to numerous tech company headquarters, including those of Parket, Admyt, ParkUpp, Xneelo, [Jumo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jumo \"Jumo\"), [Aerobotics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aerobotics \"Aerobotics\"), Cue, Vault22, [Rain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rain_(telecommunications) \"Rain (telecommunications)\"), and Luno.\n\n[Fintech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fintech \"Fintech\") is a particularly strong niche for Cape Town, and the city is home to over 60 fintech [startups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Startups \"Startups\").[[190]](#cite_note-Invest_Cape_Town_-_Economy_&_Infrastructure-192) Major fintech companies with their headquarters in Cape Town include [Yoco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoco \"Yoco\"), [Payfast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Payfast \"Payfast\"), [SnapScan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SnapScan \"SnapScan\"), [Zapper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zapper_(company) \"Zapper (company)\"), [Peach Payments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peach_Payments \"Peach Payments\"), and [Mukuru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mukuru_(South_Africa) \"Mukuru (South Africa)\").\n\nThe city also has HQs for a number of companies in the [space industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_industry \"Space industry\").[[191]](#cite_note-193)\n\nCape Town is also home to the headquarters of fibre network operators (FNOs) [Frogfoot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frogfoot_Networks \"Frogfoot Networks\") and [Octotel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Octotel \"Octotel\"). The city also houses a regional office of FNO Liquid Intelligent Technologies. A number of entrepreneurial initiatives hosting tech [startups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Startup \"Startup\"), such as the Sun Exchange, are located in the city.[[192]](#cite_note-194)[[193]](#cite_note-Cape_Town’s_Cue_raises_R82-million_to_take_AI_service_agents_global-195)\n\nIn terms of foreign tech companies, Cape Town houses the Africa HQ of ecommerce company [Amazon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amazon_(company) \"Amazon (company)\"), located at [Riverlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riverlands_(Cape_Town) \"Riverlands (Cape Town)\"), as well as the second global hub of fintech [ClearScore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ClearScore \"ClearScore\").\n\n#### Retail\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=35 \"Edit section: Retail\")]\n\nCape Town is a hub for South Africa's [retail industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Retailing_in_South_Africa \"Retailing in South Africa\") as a whole, with the city housing the headquarters of nine of South Africa's major retail companies, including all but one of its largest [supermarket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supermarket \"Supermarket\") chains.\n\nMajor brick and mortar retailers headquartered in Cape Town include [Woolworths](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woolworths_(South_Africa) \"Woolworths (South Africa)\") (including [Absolute Pets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Absolute_Pets \"Absolute Pets\")); [Pick n Pay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pick_n_Pay \"Pick n Pay\"); [Shoprite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shoprite_Holdings_Ltd \"Shoprite Holdings Ltd\") (including [Checkers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Checkers_(supermarket_chain) \"Checkers (supermarket chain)\") and [Petshop Science](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petshop_Science \"Petshop Science\")); [Pepkor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pepkor \"Pepkor\") (including [Pep](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pep_(South_Africa) \"Pep (South Africa)\") and [Ackermans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ackermans \"Ackermans\")); [MRP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mr_Price_Group \"Mr Price Group\"); [TFG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TFG_Limited \"TFG Limited\") (including Foschini, Sportscene, TotalSports, and Bash); [Clicks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clicks_(South_Africa) \"Clicks (South Africa)\") (including [Sorbet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sorbet_(South_Africa) \"Sorbet (South Africa)\")); [Truworths](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Truworths \"Truworths\"); and [Cape Union Mart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Union_Mart \"Cape Union Mart\").\n\nSouth Africa's largest online retailer, [Takealot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Takealot_Group \"Takealot Group\") (including home delivery subsidiary Mr D) has its headquarters in [Cape Town CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD \"Cape Town CBD\").\n\nOther, smaller retailers headquartered in Cape Town include [Yuppiechef](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuppiechef \"Yuppiechef\"), [Stodels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stodels \"Stodels\"), and Wootware.\n\n#### Media\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=36 \"Edit section: Media\")]\n\n[Mass media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_media \"Mass media\") is another of Cape Town's major industries. The city is home to the headquarters of numerous of South Africa's largest print and digital news publications. Some of these are part of large mass media corporations, while others are independent, like [Daily Maverick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daily_Maverick \"Daily Maverick\").\n\nThe headquarters of eMedia are located in Cape Town. eMedia operates South Africa's largest privately owned [free-to-air](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free-to-air \"Free-to-air\") [television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Television \"Television\") station, [e.tv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/E.tv \"E.tv\"), as well as major digital news publication eNCA.\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/4/4d/Naspers_Media24_building.jpg/250px-Naspers_Media24_building.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Naspers_Media24_building.jpg)\n\nThe [Media24 Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media24_Centre \"Media24 Centre\") is the headquarters of [Naspers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naspers \"Naspers\"), South Africa's largest [mass media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_media \"Mass media\") company, and the country's [third-largest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_largest_companies_in_South_Africa \"List of largest companies in South Africa\") listed company by annual revenue\n\nCape Town is also home to the headquarters of South Africa's largest mass media corporation, and the country's third-largest listed company by annual revenue, [Naspers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naspers \"Naspers\"). Located in the [Media24 Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media24_Centre \"Media24 Centre\"), Naspers owns numerous national publications through its subsidiary, [Media24](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media24 \"Media24\"). These include [News24](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/News24_(website) \"News24 (website)\")), as well as its Afrikaans sister publication, Netwerk24.\n\nOther media companies headquartered in the city include business publication company Topco Media, print media holding company [Novus Holdings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novus_Holdings \"Novus Holdings\"), and magazine publisher Highbury Safika Media. The city also has a regional office for [Primedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primedia \"Primedia\").\n\n#### Film\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=37 \"Edit section: Film\")]\n\nThe city has the largest film industry in the [Southern Hemisphere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Hemisphere \"Southern Hemisphere\")[[194]](#cite_note-196) generating R5 billion (US$476.19 million) in revenue and providing an estimated 6,058 direct and 2,502 indirect jobs in 2013.[[195]](#cite_note-197) Much of the industry is based out of the [Cape Town Film Studios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_Film_Studios \"Cape Town Film Studios\").\n\n#### Conferences\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=38 \"Edit section: Conferences\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/d/dd/Cape_Town_International_Convention_Centre_2023.jpg/250px-Cape_Town_International_Convention_Centre_2023.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Cape_Town_International_Convention_Centre_2023.jpg)\n\nThe [Cape Town International Convention Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_International_Convention_Centre \"Cape Town International Convention Centre\") (CTICC) hosts many local and international conferences and conventions\n\nIn recent years, Cape Town, has become a global [conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conference \"Conference\") hub, and a highly desirable place to host delegates for [meetings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meetings \"Meetings\"). The [Cape Town International Convention Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_International_Convention_Centre \"Cape Town International Convention Centre\") (CTICC) in Cape Town's [CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD \"Cape Town CBD\") hosts many large conferences throughout each year. The city has been increasing in popularity in this regard, and in June 2025, it was reported that Cape Town had reached 35th place globally in the International Congress and Convention Association's (ICCA) rankings of popular destinations for international association meetings.[[196]](#cite_note-Cape_Town_climbs_global_rankings,_establishing_itself_as_a_premier_destination_for_international_meetings-198)\n\nThe report highlighted that Cape Town hosted 58 such meetings in 2024, which constitutes 60% of the total hosted across South Africa that year. Cape Town also placed among the top 10 cities worldwide for average attendance per event, with an average of 717 delegates per meeting.\n\nThe Western Cape's official tourism, trade, and investment promotion agency, [Wesgro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wesgro \"Wesgro\"), stated that Cape Town and the Western Cape Convention Bureau secured 36 new conference bids in the 2024/25 financial year alone. These events are expected to generate [R](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_rand \"South African rand\")745 million in economic impact, and attract over 27,000 delegates through to 2028.[[196]](#cite_note-Cape_Town_climbs_global_rankings,_establishing_itself_as_a_premier_destination_for_international_meetings-198)\n\nIn 2026, Cape Town was named *Africa's Leading Meetings & Conference Destination* at the World Travel Awards.[[18]](#cite_note-Cape_Town_earns_double_honours_at_travel_awards-20)\n\n#### Renewable energy\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=39 \"Edit section: Renewable energy\")]\n\nThe majority of South Africa's growing [renewable energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renewable_energy \"Renewable energy\") industry is based in the Western Cape.[[197]](#cite_note-199) Numerous domestic renewable energy companies are headquartered in Cape Town, including [Charge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charge_(South_Africa) \"Charge (South Africa)\"), [Rubicon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rubicon_Group \"Rubicon Group\"), Treetops, [SOLA Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SOLA_Group \"SOLA Group\"), [Anthem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthem_(energy_company) \"Anthem (energy company)\"), [Mulilo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mulilo \"Mulilo\"), [Phelan Green Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phelan_Green_Group \"Phelan Green Group\"), [GreenCape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GreenCape \"GreenCape\"), [Red Rocket Energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Rocket_Energy \"Red Rocket Energy\"), [NOA Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NOA_Group \"NOA Group\"), African Clean Energy Developments, and G7 Renewable Energies. Foreign renewable companies with regional HQs in Cape Town include [ABO Energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ABO_Energy \"ABO Energy\"), [Acciona Energía](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acciona_Energía \"Acciona Energía\"), [ACWA Power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ACWA_Power \"ACWA Power\"), [EDF Renewables](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EDF_Renewables \"EDF Renewables\"), Globeleq, JUWI, RES, and [Mainstream Renewable Power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mainstream_Renewable_Power \"Mainstream Renewable Power\").[[198]](#cite_note-Four_companies_pioneering_renewable_energy_in_Cape_Town-200)\n\nAll in all, 15 of SA's [23 major](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renewable_energy_in_South_Africa#Energy_providers \"Renewable energy in South Africa\") renewable energy [independent power producers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_power_producers \"Independent power producers\") (IPPs), amounting to 65%, have their headquarters in Cape Town.\n\n#### Real estate\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=40 \"Edit section: Real estate\")]\n\nCape Town has the headquarters of numerous major South African [real estate investment trusts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_estate_investment_trust \"Real estate investment trust\") (REITs) and other property investment firms. These include [Equites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Equites_(company) \"Equites (company)\"), [Stor-Age Property REIT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stor-Age_Property_REIT \"Stor-Age Property REIT\"), [Spear REIT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spear_REIT \"Spear REIT\"), [FPG Property Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FPG_Property_Fund \"FPG Property Fund\"), and [Fairvest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fairvest \"Fairvest\").\n\nThe Southern African office of [RE/MAX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RE/MAX \"RE/MAX\") is also in Cape Town.\n\n#### Boat building\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=41 \"Edit section: Boat building\")]\n\nCape Town has in recent years positioned itself as a notable player in the [boat building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boat_building \"Boat building\") industry. The city has become a regional hub for the sector, and is home to numerous boat building yards. Robertson and Caine, one of the world’s largest producers of luxury [catamarans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catamaran \"Catamaran\"), is headquartered in the city, as is high-tech racing yacht company Cape Racing Yachts, and superyacht specialist Southern Wind. Cape Town is home to a skilled boat building workforce and a dedicated group of artisans in the industry, and the city produces more luxury catamarans than any other city in the world.[[199]](#cite_note-Cape_Town’s_boatbuilding_sector_sails_to_global_success-201)[[200]](#cite_note-US_tariffs_become_a_headwind_for_SA_yacht_builders-202)\n\n#### Tourism\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=42 \"Edit section: Tourism\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/Clifton_Beachs.jpg/250px-Clifton_Beachs.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Clifton_Beachs.jpg)\n\n[Clifton Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clifton,_Cape_Town \"Clifton, Cape Town\") is one of Cape Town's most famous beaches\n\n [![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d9/Ciudad_del_Cabo%2C_Kirstenbosch_NBG_29.jpg/250px-Ciudad_del_Cabo%2C_Kirstenbosch_NBG_29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Ciudad_del_Cabo,_Kirstenbosch_NBG_29.jpg)\n\n[Table Mountain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_Mountain \"Table Mountain\") from the [Kirstenbosch National Botanical Garden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirstenbosch_National_Botanical_Garden \"Kirstenbosch National Botanical Garden\")\n\n [![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/35/Boulders_Beach_2019_2.jpg/250px-Boulders_Beach_2019_2.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Boulders_Beach_2019_2.jpg)\n\n[African penguins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_penguin \"African penguin\") at [Boulders Penguin Colony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boulders_Beach \"Boulders Beach\")\n\nThe Western Cape is a highly important tourist region in South Africa; the tourism industry accounts for 9.8% of the GDP of the province and employs 9.6% of the province's workforce.[[201]](#cite_note-203)\n\nIn 2024, Cape Town had its busiest tourist season ever. Over 2.4 million tourists visited the city, spending around [R](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_rand \"South African rand\")25 billion and supporting over 106,000 jobs (around 7% of the city's total employment). Cape Town is directly connected to 31 global destinations, with 226 international flights each week during its peak tourist season.[[202]](#cite_note-How_many_jobs_does_tourism_create_in_Cape_Town?-204)\n\nCape Town is not only a popular international tourist destination in [South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa \"South Africa\"), but [Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Africa \"Africa\") as a whole. This is in part due to its mild climate, natural setting, costal geography, and well-developed infrastructure.\n\nThe city has several well-known natural features that attract tourists, most notably [Table Mountain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_Mountain \"Table Mountain\"),[[203]](#cite_note-205) which forms a large part of the [Table Mountain National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_Mountain_National_Park \"Table Mountain National Park\") and is the back end of the [City Bowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_Bowl,_Cape_Town \"City Bowl, Cape Town\"). Reaching the top of the mountain can be achieved either by hiking up, or by taking the [Table Mountain Cableway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_Mountain_Cableway \"Table Mountain Cableway\"). [Cape Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Point \"Cape Point\") is the dramatic headland at the end of the [Cape Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Peninsula \"Cape Peninsula\").[[204]](#cite_note-206)\n\nMany tourists also drive along [Chapman's Peak Drive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chapman's_Peak \"Chapman's Peak\"), a narrow road that links [Noordhoek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noordhoek,_Cape_Town \"Noordhoek, Cape Town\") with [Hout Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hout_Bay,_Cape_Town \"Hout Bay, Cape Town\"), for the views of the Atlantic Ocean and nearby mountains. It is possible to either drive or hike up [Signal Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Signal_Hill_(Cape_Town) \"Signal Hill (Cape Town)\") for closer views of the [CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD \"Cape Town CBD\"), City Bowl area, and Table Mountain.[[205]](#cite_note-207)\n\nTourists also commonly visit [Cape Town's beaches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beaches_of_Cape_Town \"Beaches of Cape Town\"), which are also very popular with local residents.[[206]](#cite_note-CTbeaches-208) It is possible to visit several different beaches in the same day, each with a different setting and atmosphere. Both coasts are popular, although the beaches in affluent [Clifton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clifton,_Cape_Town \"Clifton, Cape Town\") and elsewhere on the Atlantic Coast are better developed, with restaurants and cafés lining Beach Road, alongside the various beaches.\n\nThe [Atlantic Seaboard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_Seaboard,_Cape_Town \"Atlantic Seaboard, Cape Town\"), known as Cape Town's [Riviera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riviera \"Riviera\"), is regarded as one of the most scenic routes in South Africa; along the slopes of the Twelve Apostles to the boulders and white sand beaches of [Llandudno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Llandudno,_Cape_Town \"Llandudno, Cape Town\"), with the route ending in [Hout Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hout_Bay \"Hout Bay\"), a diverse suburb with a fishing and recreational boating harbour near a small island with a breeding colony of [African fur seals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brown_fur_seal \"Brown fur seal\").\n\nHout Bay is also accessible by road from the [Constantia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constantia,_Cape_Town \"Constantia, Cape Town\") Valley, over the mountains to the northeast, and via the picturesque [Chapman's Peak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chapman's_Peak \"Chapman's Peak\") drive, from the residential suburb of [Noordhoek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noordhoek,_Cape_Town \"Noordhoek, Cape Town\"), situated in the [False Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/False_Bay \"False Bay\") area to the south-east.[[207]](#cite_note-209) [Boulders Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boulders_Beach \"Boulders Beach\") near [Simon's Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simon's_Town \"Simon's Town\") is known for its colony of [African penguins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_penguin \"African penguin\").[[208]](#cite_note-210)\n\nThe city has a rich history, and several notable cultural attractions. Within [Cape Town CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD \"Cape Town CBD\") there are the [Artscape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artscape_Theatre_Centre \"Artscape Theatre Centre\") and [District Six Homecoming Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fugard \"Fugard\") theaters, The Houses of [Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_South_Africa \"Parliament of South Africa\"), the Iziko museums and Planetarium, the South African National Gallery, the [Castle of Good Hope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Castle_of_Good_Hope \"Castle of Good Hope\"), and [The Company's Garden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Company's_Garden \"Company's Garden\") (South Africa's oldest park). The CBD is also home to many coffeehouses, as well as numerous restaurants that provide a variety of local cuisine options.\n\nThe [Victoria & Alfred Waterfront](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_&_Alfred_Waterfront \"Victoria & Alfred Waterfront\") is a mixed-use development built on top of part of the [docks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dock_(maritime) \"Dock (maritime)\") of the [Port of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Cape_Town \"Port of Cape Town\"), and is the city's most-visited tourist attraction. It is also one of the city's most popular shopping venues, with several hundred shops as well as the [Two Oceans Aquarium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two_Oceans_Aquarium \"Two Oceans Aquarium\").[[209]](#cite_note-211)[[210]](#cite_note-Sinclair_2011-212)\n\nThe V&A also hosts the Nelson Mandela Gateway, through which [ferries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferry \"Ferry\") depart for [Robben Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robben_Island \"Robben Island\").[[211]](#cite_note-213) It is possible to take a ferry from the V&A to [Hout Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hout_Bay,_Cape_Town \"Hout Bay, Cape Town\"), [Simon's Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simon's_Town,_Cape_Town \"Simon's Town, Cape Town\") and the [Cape fur seal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_fur_seal \"Cape fur seal\") colonies on Seal and Duiker Islands. Several companies offer tours of the [Cape Flats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Flats \"Cape Flats\"), a region of mostly Coloured & Black [townships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Township_(South_Africa) \"Township (South Africa)\").[[212]](#cite_note-214)\n\nWithin the metropolitan area, the most popular areas for visitors to stay include [Camps Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camps_Bay \"Camps Bay\"), [Sea Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_Point \"Sea Point\"), the [V&A Waterfront](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_&_Alfred_Waterfront \"Victoria & Alfred Waterfront\"), the [CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD \"Cape Town CBD\"), [Hout Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hout_Bay \"Hout Bay\"), [Constantia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constantia,_Cape_Town \"Constantia, Cape Town\"), [Rondebosch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rondebosch \"Rondebosch\"), [Newlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newlands,_Cape_Town \"Newlands, Cape Town\"), and [Somerset West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Somerset_West \"Somerset West\").[[213]](#cite_note-CTtourismstats-215) In November 2013, Cape Town was voted the best global city in *[The Daily Telegraph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Daily_Telegraph \"The Daily Telegraph\")'s* annual Travel Awards.[[214]](#cite_note-216)\n\nCape Town has also attracted international celebrities in connection with film and television productions.[[215]](#cite_note-217)[[216]](#cite_note-218) In the 2010s, American singer and actress [Miley Cyrus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miley_Cyrus \"Miley Cyrus\") visited several of the city's attraction while in South Africa filming an episode of *[Black Mirror](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Mirror \"Black Mirror\").*[[217]](#cite_note-219)[[218]](#cite_note-220) While American actor [Jason Momoa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jason_Momoa \"Jason Momoa\") and [Israeli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israelis \"Israelis\") [Gal Gadot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gal_Gadot \"Gal Gadot\") discussed plans to visit and spend time in Cape Town, which they described as a new \"hub for films\".[[219]](#cite_note-221) In the 2020s, British actor [Idris Elba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Idris_Elba \"Idris Elba\") was spotted along Cape Town's shores and at the [University of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Cape_Town \"University of Cape Town\") while reportedly working with African creatives.[[220]](#cite_note-222)[[221]](#cite_note-223)\n\nCape Town offers tourists a range of air-, land-, and sea-based adventure activities, including helicopter rides, [paragliding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paragliding \"Paragliding\") and [skydiving](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skydiving \"Skydiving\"), snorkelling and [scuba diving](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scuba_diving \"Scuba diving\"), boat trips, game-fishing, hiking, mountain biking, and rock climbing. [Surfing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surfing \"Surfing\") is popular in the city, along both coasts, and Cape Town hosts the [Red Bull Big Wave Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Bull_Big_Wave_Africa \"Red Bull Big Wave Africa\") surfing competition every year. The city also has some local and international recreational scuba tourism.[[222]](#cite_note-224)\n\nThe [City of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Cape_Town \"City of Cape Town\") works closely with [Cape Town Tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_Tourism \"Cape Town Tourism\") (CTT), the body tasked with promoting tourism in the city. Being the official destination [marketing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marketing \"Marketing\") agency for Cape Town, CTT has a mandate from the city to advocate for and promote tourism in the Cape Town metropolitan area, to both local and foreign markets.\n\nThe primary focus of Cape Town Tourism is to represent and advocate for Cape Town as an appealing tourist destination, as well as to track and report on metrics related to tourism in Cape Town (which CTT does as part of its Annual Report, published online).[[223]](#cite_note-225)[[224]](#cite_note-Fin24-226) Cape Town Tourism receives a portion of its funding from the City of Cape Town while the remainder is made up of membership fees and own-generated funds.[[225]](#cite_note-227)\n\nThe [Tristan da Cunha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tristan_da_Cunha \"Tristan da Cunha\") government owns and operates a lodging facility in Cape Town which charges discounted rates to Tristan da Cunha residents and non-resident natives.[[226]](#cite_note-228) Cape Town's transport system links it to the rest of South Africa; it serves as the gateway to other destinations within the province. The [Cape Winelands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Winelands \"Cape Winelands\") and in particular the towns of [Stellenbosch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stellenbosch,_Western_Cape \"Stellenbosch, Western Cape\"), [Paarl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paarl,_Western_Cape \"Paarl, Western Cape\") and [Franschhoek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franschhoek,_Western_Cape \"Franschhoek, Western Cape\") are popular day trips from the city for sightseeing and [wine tasting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wine_tasting \"Wine tasting\").[[227]](#cite_note-229)[[228]](#cite_note-230)\n\n### Other\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=43 \"Edit section: Other\")]\n\nOther local industries include insurance, publishing, design, fashion, shipping, petrochemicals, architecture, advertising, legal, and real estate.[[229]](#cite_note-231)\n\nAside from fintech, numerous traditional [financial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finance \"Finance\") firms are based in Cape Town, including [Coronation Fund Managers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coronation_Fund_Managers \"Coronation Fund Managers\"), [Ninety One Limited](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ninety_One_Limited \"Ninety One Limited\"), [Old Mutual](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Mutual \"Old Mutual\"), [Sanlam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanlam \"Sanlam\"), [10X Investments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/10X_Investments \"10X Investments\"), [Santam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santam \"Santam\"), [Allan Gray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allan_Gray_(company) \"Allan Gray (company)\"), [Sygnia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sygnia \"Sygnia\"), [Foord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foord_Asset_Management \"Foord Asset Management\"), [Camissa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camissa_Asset_Management \"Camissa Asset Management\"), and [Taquanta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taquanta \"Taquanta\").\n\nTwo of SA's largest food manufacturers, [Pioneer Foods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pioneer_Foods \"Pioneer Foods\") (which includes [Bokomo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bokomo \"Bokomo\") products) and Oceana Group, are headquartered in Cape Town. A further two – Sea Harvest and I&J, have regional facilities in the city.\n\nIn terms of the oil and gas industry, Cape Town is home to the headquarters of [Engen Petroleum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Engen_Petroleum \"Engen Petroleum\") and [Astron Energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Astron_Energy \"Astron Energy\"), South Africa's largest and second-largest [gas station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gas_station \"Gas station\") operators by number of locations. The country's second-largest [oil refinery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_refinery \"Oil refinery\"), the [Astron Energy Refinery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Astron_Energy_Refinery \"Astron Energy Refinery\"), is also based in Cape Town.\n\nCompanies headquartered in the city that are not covered in the dedicated industry sections above include [Travelstart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Travelstart \"Travelstart\"), [Webafrica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Webafrica \"Webafrica\"), [MWEB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MWEB \"MWEB\"), [LIFT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LIFT_(airline) \"LIFT (airline)\"), [Levi Strauss South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Levi_Strauss_&_Co. \"Levi Strauss & Co.\"), [PathCare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PathCare \"PathCare\"), and [Adidas South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adidas \"Adidas\").\n\nThe city is a manufacturing base for companies including [Hisense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hisense \"Hisense\"), [GlaxoSmithKline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GlaxoSmithKline \"GlaxoSmithKline\"), [Johnson & Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johnson_&_Johnson \"Johnson & Johnson\"), and [Kimberly-Clark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kimberly-Clark \"Kimberly-Clark\").\n\nCape Town is also home to the [Atlantis Special Economic Zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantis_Special_Economic_Zone \"Atlantis Special Economic Zone\"), an industrial area focused on [green tech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_tech \"Green tech\").\n\n### Inequality\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=44 \"Edit section: Inequality\")]\n\nThe city of Cape Town's [Gini coefficient](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gini_coefficient \"Gini coefficient\") of 0.58[[230]](#cite_note-232) is lower than South Africa's Gini coefficient of 0.7. This makes it more equal than the rest of the country, including any other major South African city, although still highly unequal by international standards.[[231]](#cite_note-IOL_inequality-233)[[232]](#cite_note-Geni2000-234)\n\nBetween 2001 and 2010 the city's Gini coefficient, a measure of inequality, improved by dropping from 0.59 in 2007 to 0.57 in 2010[[233]](#cite_note-235) only to increase to 0.58 by 2017.[[234]](#cite_note-Socio-economic_profile-236)\n\n## Housing\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=45 \"Edit section: Housing\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5b/Housing_in_Somerset_West%2C_Cape_Town.jpg/330px-Housing_in_Somerset_West%2C_Cape_Town.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Housing_in_Somerset_West,_Cape_Town.jpg)\n\nModern, multi-story housing in [Somerset West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Somerset_West \"Somerset West\")\n\nCape Town has a mix of [detached](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detached_house \"Detached house\") [homes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Home \"Home\") of various sizes, [townhouses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Townhouses \"Townhouses\"), and [apartments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apartments \"Apartments\"). Certain [neighbourhoods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neighbourhoods \"Neighbourhoods\") (especially older ones and those in the [Southern Suburbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Suburbs,_Cape_Town \"Southern Suburbs, Cape Town\")) tend to be dominated by larger, detached houses on separate plots of land, with parks and tree-lined streets. Newer suburbs, and those in the [Northern Suburbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Suburbs,_Cape_Town \"Northern Suburbs, Cape Town\"), feature more of a mix of housing types, including low- and high-rise buildings and townhouse developments.[[235]](#cite_note-Cape_Town_Property_Trends_and_Statistics-237)\n\n### Home ownership\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=46 \"Edit section: Home ownership\")]\n\nThe city has for years had the most expensive [average home price in South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Housing_in_South_Africa \"Housing in South Africa\"). In September 2024, the average [residential](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Residential \"Residential\") property price in Cape Town was [R](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_rand \"South African rand\")2.23 million. This was an increase of 36% from the average price of R1.63 million in 2014 (a decade before), indicating consistently strong returns on [investment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Investment \"Investment\") for those in the Cape Town property market. Sellers often get their asking price, or within 5% of it.[[236]](#cite_note-Airbnb_trend_hiking_property_sales_in_Cape_Town's_CBD-238)[[237]](#cite_note-Dark_clouds_for_Cape_Town_property_and_its_residents-239)\n\nAs of 2021, South Africa's upmarket real estate segment covered homes worth more than R1.5 million. Most of the country's high-value [residential](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Residential \"Residential\") properties are located in the Western Cape, of which Cape Town is the capital.[[238]](#cite_note-National_Housing_Market_Report,_2021-240)\n\nCape Town's property market grows at a rate far exceeding that of other major metropolitan areas in the country.[[20]](#cite_note-South_Africans_flock_to_Cape_Town-22) For example, Statistics South Africa (Stats SA) reported that between January 2010 and November 2022, Cape Town property prices increased at around double the rate of those in Johannesburg.[[239]](#cite_note-Stats_SA's_new_property_price_index:_Cape_Town_has_become_more_expensive-241) The city has been ranked as the world's second-strongest-performing real estate market.[[240]](#cite_note-Cape_Town_residential_property_market_ranked_world's_second_top-performing_market-242)\n\nThis performance improved further in more recent years. In June 2025, it was reported in South Africa's Residential Property Price Index (RPPI) that Cape Town's property values had not only increased by significantly more than the national average, but had also increased by almost 4 times as much as second-place Johannesburg's.[[241]](#cite_note-Cape_Town_continues_to_lead_national_house_price_growth-243)\n\nTo afford the average home in Cape Town, a salary of over R80,000 per month is required, which is significantly higher than in other provinces, and around 3 times the national average salary in South Africa.[[242]](#cite_note-56_jobs_that_pay_more_than_the_average_salary_of_R28,300_in_South_Africa-244) The [Western Cape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Cape \"Western Cape\") province in general requires far higher salaries for affording to purchase the average property than in other provinces.[[243]](#cite_note-Here's_what_it_takes_to_afford_a_home_in_South_Africa-245)\n\nProperty in the [Southern Suburbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Suburbs,_Cape_Town \"Southern Suburbs, Cape Town\") offers a peaceful lifestyle and is generally more expensive, as is property in the [Atlantic Seaboard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_Seaboard,_Cape_Town \"Atlantic Seaboard, Cape Town\") area, for its ocean views, and property in the [City Bowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_Bowl \"City Bowl\") region, for its proximity to [Cape Town's CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD \"Cape Town CBD\") and amenities. Conversely, property in the [Cape Flats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Flats \"Cape Flats\") is cheaper on average, as the region is less desirable to live in. The same goes for property in the [Northern Suburbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Suburbs,_Cape_Town \"Northern Suburbs, Cape Town\") region, where lower costs are as a result of living further from central Cape Town, and more space to build outwards (both inland and up the west coast).\n\nSome suburbs in Cape Town have had exceptionally high property value growth in recent years. For example, in 2025, it was reported that [Kalk Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalk_Bay \"Kalk Bay\"), an old fishing village in the [False Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/False_Bay \"False Bay\") area of Cape Town, had its house prices grow by around R1 million every year since 2020, due to an increase in demand for property in the Western Cape. The average price of a home in the suburb rose from R4.9 million in 2020 to R9 million at the start of 2025; an 83% increase over that 4-year period, or over 20% per year.[[244]](#cite_note-The_South_African_fishing_village_where_house_prices_increased_by_R1_million_every_year_since_2020-246)[[245]](#cite_note-One_area_where_house_prices_increased_by_over_R4_million_in_5_years-247)\n\nLikewise, the small town of [Scarborough](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scarborough,_Western_Cape \"Scarborough, Western Cape\"), which forms part of the [City of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Cape_Town \"City of Cape Town\") and is popular with foreign home buyers, saw a 241% increase in property values over a 9-year period, with average home values rising from R2.30 million in 2016 to R7.85 million in 2025. One reason for this kind of housing price growth in Cape Town is the local municipality's investments in maintaining and upgrading neighbourhoods, to create an attractive environment for residents to live in.[[244]](#cite_note-The_South_African_fishing_village_where_house_prices_increased_by_R1_million_every_year_since_2020-246)[[245]](#cite_note-One_area_where_house_prices_increased_by_over_R4_million_in_5_years-247)[[246]](#cite_note-Foreigners_flocking_to_buy_property_in_this_tiny_seaside_village_in_South_Africa-248)\n\nProperty prices in Cape Town are around double the national average of R1.41 million, according to home loan statistics. In the fourth quarter of 2023, the national average home price recorded its second consecutive decrease. Meanwhile, Cape Town property prices have continued to increase.[[247]](#cite_note-What_the_average_house_price_in_South_Africa_gets_you_in_Joburg,_Durban_and_Cape_Town-249)[[248]](#cite_note-Super_luxury_home-sales_boom,_Joburg_buyers_pay_more_for_Cape_Town_houses-250)\n\nCape Town's property values increase on average around 10% per year. That figure was 11.5% year-over-year in January 2026.[[249]](#cite_note-People_and_businesses_are_moving_to_where_things_just_work_in_South_Africa-251) The consistently strong property prices and growth thereof can be attributed to numerous factors, including good service delivery by [local government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Cape_Town \"City of Cape Town\"), a strong local
