\"Worcester, South Africa\").[[356]](#cite_note-:02-358)\n\nThe [N2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N2_(South_Africa) \"N2 (South Africa)\") begins at the foreshore area near the Cape Town city centre and runs east-south-east through [Mowbray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mowbray,_Cape_Town \"Mowbray, Cape Town\"), [Khayelitsha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khayelitsha \"Khayelitsha\"), [Macassar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macassar,_Western_Cape \"Macassar, Western Cape\") and [Somerset West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Somerset_West \"Somerset West\") before leaving the municipality as the [Sir Lowry's Pass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sir_Lowry's_Pass \"Sir Lowry's Pass\"), connecting with coastal cities to the east, including [Mossel Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mossel_Bay \"Mossel Bay\"), [George](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George,_South_Africa \"George, South Africa\"), [Port Elizabeth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Elizabeth \"Port Elizabeth\"), [East London](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_London,_South_Africa \"East London, South Africa\") and [Durban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durban \"Durban\").[[356]](#cite_note-:02-358) An older at-grade road, the [R102](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R102_(South_Africa) \"R102 (South Africa)\"), runs parallel to the N1 initially, before veering south-east at Bellville, to join the N2 at Somerset West via the towns of [Kuilsrivier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuils_River \"Kuils River\") and [Eersterivier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eerste_River,_Western_Cape \"Eerste River, Western Cape\").[[356]](#cite_note-:02-358)\n\nThe [N7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N7_(South_Africa) \"N7 (South Africa)\") originates from the N1 at Wingfield Interchange near [Acacia Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acacia_Park,_Cape_Town \"Acacia Park, Cape Town\") and [Edgemead](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edgemead \"Edgemead\"). It heads northwards, initially as a highway, but becomes an at-grade road from the intersection with the [M12](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M12_(Cape_Town) \"M12 (Cape Town)\") onwards. It follows the Atlantic coast northwards towards [Malmesbury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malmesbury \"Malmesbury\"), [Clanwilliam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clanwilliam,_South_Africa \"Clanwilliam, South Africa\"), [Vredendal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vredendal \"Vredendal\"), [Springbok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Springbok,_South_Africa \"Springbok, South Africa\") and the border with [Namibia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Namibia \"Namibia\").[[356]](#cite_note-:02-358)\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8f/Cape_Town_N2.jpg/250px-Cape_Town_N2.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Cape_Town_N2.jpg)\n\nThe [N2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N2_(South_Africa) \"N2 (South Africa)\") as it enters [Cape Town CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD \"Cape Town CBD\")\n\nThere are also a number of [provincial routes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provincial_routes_(South_Africa) \"Provincial routes (South Africa)\") linking Cape Town with surrounding areas. The [R27](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R27_road_(South_Africa) \"R27 road (South Africa)\") originates from the N1 near the Foreshore and runs north parallel to the N7, but nearer to the coast. It passes through the suburbs of [Milnerton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milnerton,_Cape_Town \"Milnerton, Cape Town\"), [Table View](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_View,_Cape_Town \"Table View, Cape Town\") and [Bloubergstrand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bloubergstrand,_Cape_Town \"Bloubergstrand, Cape Town\") and links the city to the West Coast, ending at the town of [Velddrif](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Velddrif \"Velddrif\").[[356]](#cite_note-:02-358)\n\nThe [R44](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R44_road_(South_Africa) \"R44 road (South Africa)\") enters the east of the metro from the north, from [Stellenbosch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stellenbosch \"Stellenbosch\"). It connects Stellenbosch to Somerset West, then crosses the N2 to [Strand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strand,_Western_Cape \"Strand, Western Cape\") and [Gordon's Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gordon's_Bay \"Gordon's Bay\"). It exits the metro heading south hugging the coast, leading to the towns of [Betty's Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Betty's_Bay \"Betty's Bay\") and [Kleinmond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kleinmond \"Kleinmond\").[[356]](#cite_note-:02-358)\n\nThere are also a number of [regional routes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regional_routes_(South_Africa) \"Regional routes (South Africa)\") (\"R\" routes) in the area. The [R300](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R300_road_(South_Africa) \"R300 road (South Africa)\") is an expressway linking the N1 at [Brackenfell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brackenfell \"Brackenfell\") to the N2 near [Mitchells Plain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mitchells_Plain \"Mitchells Plain\") and the [Cape Town International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_International_Airport \"Cape Town International Airport\").[[356]](#cite_note-:02-358) The [R302](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R302_road_(South_Africa) \"R302 road (South Africa)\") runs from the R102 in Bellville, heading north across the N1 through [Durbanville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durbanville \"Durbanville\") before leaving the metro to reach [Malmesbury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malmesbury,_Western_Cape \"Malmesbury, Western Cape\").[[356]](#cite_note-:02-358)\n\nThe [R304](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R304_road_(South_Africa) \"R304 road (South Africa)\") enters the northern limits of the metro from Stellenbosch, running north-north-west before veering west to cross the N7 at [Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia,_Western_Cape \"Philadelphia, Western Cape\") to end at [Atlantis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantis,_Western_Cape \"Atlantis, Western Cape\") at a junction with the [R307](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R307_road_(South_Africa) \"R307 road (South Africa)\").[[356]](#cite_note-:02-358) This R307 starts north of [Koeberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koeberg \"Koeberg\") from the R27 and, after meeting the R304, continues north to [Darling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Darling,_Western_Cape \"Darling, Western Cape\").[[356]](#cite_note-:02-358) The [R310](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R310_road_(South_Africa) \"R310 road (South Africa)\") originates from [Muizenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muizenberg \"Muizenberg\") and runs along the coast, to the south of Mitchell's Plain and Khayelitsha, before veering north-east, crossing the N2 west of Macassar, and exiting the metro heading to Stellenbosch.[[356]](#cite_note-:02-358)\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/31/Road_Infrastructure_in_Tokai%2C_Cape_Town.jpg/330px-Road_Infrastructure_in_Tokai%2C_Cape_Town.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Road_Infrastructure_in_Tokai,_Cape_Town.jpg)\n\nRoad infrastructure in the suburb of [Dreyersdal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dreyersdal \"Dreyersdal\")\n\nCape Town, like most South African cities, uses [Metropolitan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_Routes_in_Cape_Town \"Metropolitan Routes in Cape Town\") or \"M\" routes for important intra-city routes, a layer below National (N) roads and Regional (R) routes. Each city's M roads are independently numbered. Most are at-grade roads. The [M3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M3_(Cape_Town) \"M3 (Cape Town)\") splits from the N2 and runs to the south along the eastern slopes of Table Mountain, connecting the [CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD \"Cape Town CBD\") with [Muizenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muizenberg \"Muizenberg\"). Except for a section between Rondebosch and Newlands that has at-grade intersections, this route is a highway. The [M5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M5_(Cape_Town) \"M5 (Cape Town)\") splits from the N1 further east than the M3, and links the Cape Flats to the CBD. It is a highway as far as the interchange with the M68 at Ottery, before continuing as an at-grade road. Cape Town has the worst traffic congestion in [South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa \"South Africa\") as of 2015.[[357]](#cite_note-359)[[358]](#cite_note-360)\n\n#### Licensing\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=71 \"Edit section: Licensing\")]\n\nVehicles registered in the City of Cape Town metro use [license plates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/License_plate \"License plate\") denoting the subdivision of registration, from a group allocated by the [provincial government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_the_Western_Cape \"Government of the Western Cape\"). Due to population spread, most plates use \"CA\", while newer vehicles in Cape Town use \"CAA\", which was introduced in April 2019. Other regions include \"CY\" for [Durbanville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durbanville \"Durbanville\") and surrounds, \"CF\" for [Kuils River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuils_River \"Kuils River\") and surrounds, and \"CFM\" for [Somerset West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Somerset_West \"Somerset West\") and surrounds. As with the rest of the [Western Cape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Cape \"Western Cape\") province, and as opposed to the original letter-number plate format, the most recent license plates issued in Cape Town use the format of two letters, two figures, and two letters (in any sequence), followed by \"WC\" (for Western Cape).[[359]](#cite_note-This_is_what_Cape_Town’s_new_number_plates_will_look_like-361)\n\nIn terms of [driver's licensing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drivers_license \"Drivers license\"), Cape Town, and the [Western Cape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Cape \"Western Cape\") province, are significantly more advanced than the rest of South Africa. In May 2025, the Western Cape launched a digital [learner's license](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Learner's_permit \"Learner's permit\") testing system, the goal of which was to replace the traditional pen and paper test, reduce corruption, and deliver faster and more accurate results.[[360]](#cite_note-Western_Cape_launches_digital_learner's_license_testing_system-362)\n\nIn September 2025, Cape Town launched its drive through driver's license renewal system, with a facility in [Brackenfell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brackenfell \"Brackenfell\"). The system allows for rapid renewal (in under 5 minutes), without driver's leaving their cars. Renewals can be sped up further if driver's have received an SMS renewal confirmation, and show it at the drive through. The first facility is [cashless](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cashless \"Cashless\"), and operates Monday through Saturday.[[361]](#cite_note-Cape_Town_launches_first_vehicle_licensing_renewal_drive-through_facility-363)\n\n### Buses\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=72 \"Edit section: Buses\")]\n\nCity-owned MyCiTi and privately owned Golden Arrow both operate scheduled, metro-wide bus services. Several other companies run long-distance bus services between Cape Town and other South African cities.\n\n#### Golden Arrow\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=73 \"Edit section: Golden Arrow\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Golden_Arrow_Electric_Bus%2C_by_BYD%2C_in_Kirstenhof%2C_Cape_Town.jpg/250px-Golden_Arrow_Electric_Bus%2C_by_BYD%2C_in_Kirstenhof%2C_Cape_Town.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Golden_Arrow_Electric_Bus,_by_BYD,_in_Kirstenhof,_Cape_Town.jpg)\n\n[Golden Arrow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Arrow_Bus_Service \"Golden Arrow Bus Service\") [electric bus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_bus \"Electric bus\"), by BYD, in Kirstenhof, Cape Town\n\n[Golden Arrow Bus Services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Arrow_Bus_Services \"Golden Arrow Bus Services\") (GABS) provides commuter bus services throughout a large portion of Cape Town, and operates a fleet of around 1,200 electric and diesel buses on 1,300 routes, with a daily ridership of around 220,000 people.[[362]](#cite_note-Home-364)\n\nThe network uses an [NFC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Near_field_communication \"Near field communication\") Gold Card for cashless payments, and offers discounted fares for weekly and monthly passes, as well as for students and pensioners.[[363]](#cite_note-Golden_Arrow_Bus_Service_-_Homepage-365)\n\nGABS operates 7 hubs, situated across the [City of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Cape_Town \"City of Cape Town\").[[364]](#cite_note-About_Us-366)\n\nGolden Arrow was the first bus operator in South Africa to roll out [electric buses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_bus \"Electric bus\"), via a partnership with BYD. The first fleet members entered operation in 2024, with more launched in 2025, alongside an expanded charging network.[[365]](#cite_note-Western_Cape_rolls_out_new_BYD_electric_buses-367)[[366]](#cite_note-BYD_Pioneers_South_Africa’s_Electric_Public_Transport_with_120-Electric-Bus_Deal-368)\n\n#### MyCiTi\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=74 \"Edit section: MyCiTi\")]\n\nMain article: [MyCiTi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MyCiTi \"MyCiTi\")\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/MyCiTi_bus.jpg/250px-MyCiTi_bus.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:MyCiTi_bus.jpg)\n\nA MyCiTi [Optare Solo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Optare_Solo \"Optare Solo\") bus\n\nThe MyCiTi [bus rapid transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bus_rapid_transit \"Bus rapid transit\") (BRT) system is extensive, and operated directly by the [City of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Cape_Town \"City of Cape Town\"). It forms a major part of the city's overall public transit system.[[367]](#cite_note-The_next_big_step_for_MyCiTi_is_Khayelitsha_and_Mitchells_Plain-369)\n\nThe system has a fleet of 223 [electric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_bus \"Electric bus\") and [diesel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diesel_fuel \"Diesel fuel\") buses, of various sizes. They operate between 42 stations, and around 1,000 stops throughout Cape Town.[[368]](#cite_note-Integrated_Annual_Report_2023/2024-370)\n\nThe service operates on a combination of dedicated [bus lanes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bus_lane \"Bus lane\") (painted red and featuring dedicated bus traffic signals), and traditional, mixed-use roadways.\n\nStations feature free Wi-Fi, enclosed waiting areas, and off-grid power. MyCiTi requires cashless fare payment using mobile [tap-to-pay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tap-to-pay \"Tap-to-pay\"), or the company's [EMV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EMV \"EMV\")-compliant card, called myconnect.\n\nThe MyCiTi BRT system is being rolled out across four phases. As of 2026, Phase 1A, 1B, and the N2 Corridor have been completed, and Phase 2A is underway.[[369]](#cite_note-Phase_2A-371) Phase 2A is the largest investment in public transit by any metro in South Africa in history, and features the construction of an electric bus charging depot, as well as South Africa's first elevated traffic circle.[[370]](#cite_note-Cape_Town’s_MyCiti_gets_big_boost_amid_expansion_plans-372)\n\nA procurement agreement has been signed with [Volvo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volvo \"Volvo\") Bus Southern Africa for electric buses, with initial deliveries scheduled for 2027.[[371]](#cite_note-City_of_Cape_Town_to_procure_30_electric_buses_from_Volvo-373)\n\n### Taxis\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=75 \"Edit section: Taxis\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a2/Cape_Town_Taxicab_in_Campsbay.jpg/250px-Cape_Town_Taxicab_in_Campsbay.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Cape_Town_Taxicab_in_Campsbay.jpg)\n\nA fleet of privately owned taxis in [Camps Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camps_Bay \"Camps Bay\")\n\nCape Town has various kinds of taxis available, including those obtained through [ride-hailing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ride-hailing \"Ride-hailing\") (app-based) services such as [Bolt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolt_(company) \"Bolt (company)\") and [Uber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uber \"Uber\"), as well as traditional metered taxies.\n\nThe [Western Cape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Cape \"Western Cape\") Provincial Regulatory Entity (PRE) issues ride-hailing licenses for Cape Town, which have expiry dates and need to be renewed. They are processed in the order they are received, and the PRE ensures drivers are operating legally. As of mid-2025, the city has 5,494 registered ride-hailing licenses.[[372]](#cite_note-Good_news_for_people_who_use_Uber_and_Bolt_in_Cape_Town-374)\n\nTraditional taxis are either [metered taxis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taxicab \"Taxicab\") or [minibus taxis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minibus_taxi \"Minibus taxi\"). Metered taxis can be found at transport hubs as well as other tourist establishments, while minibus taxis can be found at taxi ranks, and, commonly, travelling along main streets.[[373]](#cite_note-375) Minibus taxis can be hailed from the road.[[374]](#cite_note-376)\n\nCape Town metered taxi cabs mostly operate in CBD and Cape Town International Airport areas. Large companies that operate fleets of cabs can be reached by phone and are cheaper than the single operators that apply for hire from taxi ranks and [Victoria and Alfred Waterfront](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_and_Alfred_Waterfront \"Victoria and Alfred Waterfront\").\n\nThere are about 1,000 meter taxis in Cape Town. Their rates vary from R8 per kilometre to about R15 per kilometre. The larger taxi companies in Cape Town are Excite Taxis, Cabnet and Intercab and single operators are reachable by cellular phone. The seven seated Toyota Avanza are the most popular with larger Taxi companies. Meter cabs are mostly used by tourists and are safer to use than minibus taxis.[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed \"Wikipedia:Citation needed\")*]\n\nMinibus taxis are the standard form of transport for the majority of the population who cannot afford private vehicles.[[375]](#cite_note-377) Although essential, these taxis are often poorly maintained and are frequently not road-worthy. These taxis make frequent unscheduled stops to pick up passengers, which can cause accidents.[[376]](#cite_note-378)[[377]](#cite_note-379) With the high demand for transport by the working class of South Africa, minibus taxis are often filled over their legal passenger allowance. Minibuses are generally owned and operated in fleets.[[378]](#cite_note-380)\n\n## Culture\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=76 \"Edit section: Culture\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/98/Groot_Constantia_-_2.jpg/250px-Groot_Constantia_-_2.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Groot_Constantia_-_2.jpg)\n\n[Groot Constantia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Groot_Constantia \"Groot Constantia\"), an example of Cape Dutch architecture.\n\n [![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3b/Mosterts_Mill.jpg/250px-Mosterts_Mill.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Mosterts_Mill.jpg)\n\n[Mostert's Mill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mostert's_Mill \"Mostert's Mill\") in Cape Town.\n\nCape Town is noted for its architectural heritage, with the highest density of [Cape Dutch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Dutch_architecture \"Cape Dutch architecture\") style buildings in the world. Cape Dutch style, which combines the architectural traditions of the Netherlands, Germany, France and Indonesia, is most visible in [Constantia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constantia,_Cape_Town \"Constantia, Cape Town\"), the [old government buildings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houses_of_Parliament,_Cape_Town \"Houses of Parliament, Cape Town\") in the Central Business District, and along [Long Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Long_Street_(Cape_Town) \"Long Street (Cape Town)\").[[379]](#cite_note-381)[[380]](#cite_note-382) The only complete windmill in South Africa is [Mostert's Mill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mostert's_Mill \"Mostert's Mill\"), [Mowbray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mowbray,_Cape_Town \"Mowbray, Cape Town\"). It was built in 1796 and restored in 1935 and again in 1995.\n\nCape Town was named the [World Design Capital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Design_Capital \"World Design Capital\") for 2014 by the [International Council of Societies of Industrial Design](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Council_of_Societies_of_Industrial_Design \"International Council of Societies of Industrial Design\").[[381]](#cite_note-383) Cape Town is also recognized as a \"[Design City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Design_Cities_(UNESCO) \"Design Cities (UNESCO)\")\" by [UNESCO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNESCO \"UNESCO\")'s [Creative Cities Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Creative_Cities_Network \"Creative Cities Network\"). The [Artscape Theatre Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artscape_Theatre_Centre \"Artscape Theatre Centre\") is the largest performing arts venue in Cape Town.[[382]](#cite_note-384)\n\nIn 2026, Cape Town was named the 5th-best city in the world for arts and culture, by UK's [*Time Out*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_Out_(magazine) \"Time Out (magazine)\") magazine.[[383]](#cite_note-Cape_Town_voted_one_of_the_world's_best_cities_for_culture!-385)\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/05/Kaapse_Klopse_marching_through_Cape_Town_%282017%29.jpg/250px-Kaapse_Klopse_marching_through_Cape_Town_%282017%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Kaapse_Klopse_marching_through_Cape_Town_(2017).jpg)\n\n[Cape Town Minstrel Carnival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaapse_Klopse \"Kaapse Klopse\") (2017).\n\n [![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/68/Holi_One_We_Are_One_Colour_Festival_South_Africa_2013.jpg/250px-Holi_One_We_Are_One_Colour_Festival_South_Africa_2013.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Holi_One_We_Are_One_Colour_Festival_South_Africa_2013.jpg)\n\n[Holi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holi \"Holi\") festival at the [Grand Parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Parade_(Cape_Town) \"Grand Parade (Cape Town)\").\n\nThe annual [Cape Town Minstrel Carnival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coon_Carnival \"Coon Carnival\"), also known by its Afrikaans name of *Kaapse Klopse*, is a large [minstrel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minstrel \"Minstrel\") festival held annually on 2 January or *\"Tweede Nuwe Jaar\"* (Second New Year). Competing teams of minstrels parade in brightly coloured costumes, performing [Cape Jazz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Jazz \"Cape Jazz\"), either carrying colourful umbrellas or playing an array of musical instruments.\n\nThe city also encloses the 36 hectare [Kirstenbosch National Botanical Garden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirstenbosch_National_Botanical_Garden \"Kirstenbosch National Botanical Garden\") that contains protected natural forest and [fynbos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fynbos \"Fynbos\") along with a variety of animals and birds. There are over 7,000 species in cultivation at Kirstenbosch, including many rare and threatened species of the [Cape Floristic Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Floristic_Region \"Cape Floristic Region\"). In 2004 this Region, including Kirstenbosch, was declared a UNESCO [World Heritage Site](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Heritage_Site \"World Heritage Site\").[[384]](#cite_note-386)\n\n[Whale watching](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whale_watching \"Whale watching\") is popular amongst tourists: [southern right whales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_right_whale \"Southern right whale\") and [humpback whales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humpback_whale \"Humpback whale\") are seen off the coast during the breeding season (August to November) and [Bryde's whales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bryde's_whale \"Bryde's whale\") and [orca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orca \"Orca\") can be seen any time of the year.[[385]](#cite_note-CTwhale-387) The nearby town of [Hermanus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hermanus \"Hermanus\") is known for its Whale Festival, but whales can also be seen in False Bay.[[385]](#cite_note-CTwhale-387) [Heaviside's dolphins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heaviside's_dolphin \"Heaviside's dolphin\") are endemic to the area and can be seen from the coast north of Cape Town; [dusky dolphins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dusky_dolphin \"Dusky dolphin\") live along the same coast and can occasionally be seen from the ferry to Robben Island.[[385]](#cite_note-CTwhale-387)\n\n### Cuisine\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=77 \"Edit section: Cuisine\")]\n\nSee also: [South African cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_cuisine \"South African cuisine\")\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c5/Cape_Town_-_Vegan_Lekker_Nuggy_Gatsby_in_restaurant_Lekker_Vegan.jpg/250px-Cape_Town_-_Vegan_Lekker_Nuggy_Gatsby_in_restaurant_Lekker_Vegan.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Cape_Town_-_Vegan_Lekker_Nuggy_Gatsby_in_restaurant_Lekker_Vegan.jpg)\n\nA gatsby sandwich, freshly prepared\n\nFood originating from or synonymous with Cape Town includes the savoury sweet spiced meat dish [Bobotie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bobotie \"Bobotie\") that dates from the 17th century. The [Gatsby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gatsby_(sandwich) \"Gatsby (sandwich)\"), a sandwich filled with [slap chips](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slap_chips \"Slap chips\") and other toppings, was first served in 1976 in the suburb of [Athlone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athlone,_Cape_Town \"Athlone, Cape Town\") and is also synonymous with the city.[[386]](#cite_note-388) The [koe'sister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koe'sister \"Koe'sister\") is a traditional [Cape Malay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Malays \"Cape Malays\") pastry described as a cinnamon infused dumpling with a cake-like texture, finished off with a sprinkling of desiccated coconut.[[387]](#cite_note-389)\n\n[Malva pudding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malva_pudding \"Malva pudding\") (sometimes known as Cape Malva pudding) is a sticky sweet dessert often served with hot custard is also associated with the city and dates back to the 17th century.[[388]](#cite_note-390) A related dessert dish, [Cape Brandy Pudding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Brandy_Pudding \"Cape Brandy Pudding\"), is also associated with the city and surrounding region.[[389]](#cite_note-391) Cape Town is also the home of the [South African wine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_wine \"South African wine\") industry with the first wine produced in the country being bottled in the city; a number of notable wineries still exist in the city including [Groot Constantia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Groot_Constantia \"Groot Constantia\") and [Klein Constantia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klein_Constantia \"Klein Constantia\").\n\nThe city has gained international recognition for its [pizza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pizza \"Pizza\") scene. In 2025, then-new, family-owned restaurant NOVO, based in [Mowbray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mowbray,_Cape_Town \"Mowbray, Cape Town\"), ranked in publication *Time Out'*s Top 20 Pizzas globally. It did so for its \"The Fellini\" creation.[[390]](#cite_note-A_Cape_Town_pizza_has_been_named_in_top_19_globally-392) In the 2026 ranking from the same publication, [Woodstock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woodstock,_Cape_Town \"Woodstock, Cape Town\")-based establishment Pizza Connection ranked 11th on the list, for its \"The Capetonian\" pizza.[[391]](#cite_note-Cape_Town_pizzeria_bags_global_top_spot-393)\n\n[Coffee culture in Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coffee_culture_in_Cape_Town \"Coffee culture in Cape Town\") is thriving, with many independent coffeehouses and cafe chains with locations across the city. All four of South Africa's largest cafe chains, [Vida e Caffè](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vida_e_Caffè \"Vida e Caffè\"), [Seattle Coffee Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Coffee_Company \"Seattle Coffee Company\"), [WCafe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woolworths_(South_Africa) \"Woolworths (South Africa)\"), and [Bootlegger Coffee Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bootlegger_Coffee_Company \"Bootlegger Coffee Company\"), are headquartered in Cape Town. Furthermore, the city is home to 10 roasteries.\n\nCape Town is considered one of the best cities in the world for coffee,[[392]](#cite_note-Cape_Town_brews_success:_Named_one_of_world's_top_cities_for_coffee-394)[[393]](#cite_note-Cape_Town's_Coffee_Culture-395) and is known as an international coffee capital.[[394]](#cite_note-No_Sleep_for_Cape_Town's_Thriving_Coffee_Culture-396)[[395]](#cite_note-Coffee_culture_flourishes_in_downtown_Cape_Town-397) In 2024, Cape Town was ranked as the world's 9th best city for coffee by US *Food & Wine Magazine*.[[392]](#cite_note-Cape_Town_brews_success:_Named_one_of_world's_top_cities_for_coffee-394) Truth Coffee, situated in [Cape Town CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD \"Cape Town CBD\"), was voted Best Coffee Shop in the World in 2015 and 2016 by the UK's *[Daily Telegraph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Daily_Telegraph \"The Daily Telegraph\")*[[395]](#cite_note-Coffee_culture_flourishes_in_downtown_Cape_Town-397) and Espresso Lab in [Woodstock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woodstock,_Cape_Town \"Woodstock, Cape Town\") was voted the 11th best coffee shop in the world, and the best in Africa, in 2025.[[396]](#cite_note-You_can_visit_one_of_the_world's_best_coffee_shops_right_here_in_Cape_Town-398)\n\n### Media\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=78 \"Edit section: Media\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fe/Cape_Town_TV_news_van.jpg/250px-Cape_Town_TV_news_van.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Cape_Town_TV_news_van.jpg)\n\nA news van owned by [Cape Town TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_TV \"Cape Town TV\") outside the [Houses of Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houses_of_Parliament,_Cape_Town \"Houses of Parliament, Cape Town\")\n\nSeveral newspapers, magazines and printing facilities have their offices in the city. [Independent News and Media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_News_and_Media \"Independent News and Media\") publishes the major English language papers in the city, the *[Cape Argus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Argus \"Cape Argus\")* and the *[Cape Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Times \"Cape Times\")*. [Naspers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naspers \"Naspers\"), the largest media conglomerate in South Africa, publishes *[Die Burger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Die_Burger \"Die Burger\")*, the major Afrikaans language paper.[[397]](#cite_note-399)\n\nCape Town has many local community [newspapers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newspapers \"Newspapers\"). Some of the largest community newspapers in English are the *Athlone News* from [Athlone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athlone,_Cape_Town \"Athlone, Cape Town\"), the *[Atlantic Sun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_Sun \"Atlantic Sun\")*, the *Constantiaberg Bulletin* from [Constantiaberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constantiaberg,_Cape_Town \"Constantiaberg, Cape Town\"), the *City Vision* from [Bellville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bellville,_Cape_Town \"Bellville, Cape Town\"), the *[False Bay Echo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/False_Bay_Echo \"False Bay Echo\")* from False Bay, Bolander (formerly known as the Helderberg Sun) from [Helderberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helderberg,_Cape_Town \"Helderberg, Cape Town\"), the *[Plainsman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plainsman_(South_Africa) \"Plainsman (South Africa)\")* from Michell's Plain, the *[Sentinel News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sentinel_News \"Sentinel News\")* from Hout Bay, the *[Southern Mail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Mail_(newspaper) \"Southern Mail (newspaper)\")* from the Southern Peninsula, the *[Southern Suburbs Tatler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Suburbs_Tatler \"Southern Suburbs Tatler\")* from the [Southern Suburbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Suburbs,_Cape_Town \"Southern Suburbs, Cape Town\"), *[Table Talk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_Talk_(Cape_Town) \"Table Talk (Cape Town)\")* from Table View and *Tygertalk* from Tygervalley/Durbanville. Afrikaans language community newspapers include the *[Landbou-Burger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Landbou-Burger \"Landbou-Burger\")* and the *Tygerburger*. *[Vukani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vukani \"Vukani\")*, based in the Cape Flats, is published in [Xhosa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xhosa_language \"Xhosa language\").[[398]](#cite_note-400)\n\nCape Town is a centre for major broadcast media with several radio stations that only broadcast within the city. [94.5 Kfm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KFM_(Cape_Town) \"KFM (Cape Town)\") (94.5 [MHz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MHz \"MHz\") FM) and [Good Hope FM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Good_Hope_FM \"Good Hope FM\") (94–97 MHz [FM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FM_broadcasting \"FM broadcasting\")) mostly play [pop music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pop_music \"Pop music\"). Heart FM (104.9 MHz FM), the former P4 Radio, plays [jazz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jazz \"Jazz\") and [R&B](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contemporary_R&B \"Contemporary R&B\"), while [Fine Music Radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fine_Music_Radio \"Fine Music Radio\") (101.3 FM) plays [classical music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_classical_music \"European classical music\") and jazz, and Magic Music Radio[[399]](#cite_note-401) (828 [kHz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kilohertz \"Kilohertz\") MW) plays adult contemporary and classic rock from the '80s, '90s and '00s.[[400]](#cite_note-402)\n\n[Bush Radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bush_Radio_(South_Africa) \"Bush Radio (South Africa)\") is a community radio station (89.5 MHz FM). The [Voice of the Cape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voice_of_the_Cape \"Voice of the Cape\") (95.8 MHz FM) and [Cape Talk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Talk \"Cape Talk\") (567 kHz [MW](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mediumwave \"Mediumwave\")) are the major [talk radio stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talk_radio \"Talk radio\") in the city.[[401]](#cite_note-403) Bokradio (98.9 MHz FM) is an Afrikaans music station.[[402]](#cite_note-404) The [University of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Cape_Town \"University of Cape Town\") also runs its own radio station, [UCT Radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UCT_Radio \"UCT Radio\") (104.5 MHz FM).\n\nThe [SABC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SABC \"SABC\") has a small presence in the city, with satellite studios located at [Sea Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_Point \"Sea Point\"). [e.tv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/E.tv \"E.tv\") has a greater presence, with a large complex located at Longkloof Studios in [Gardens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gardens,_Cape_Town \"Gardens, Cape Town\"). [M-Net](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M-Net \"M-Net\") is not well represented with infrastructure within the city. [Cape Town TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_TV \"Cape Town TV\") is a local TV station, supported by numerous organisation and focusing mostly on documentaries.\n\nNumerous productions companies and their support industries are located in the city, mostly supporting the production of overseas commercials, model shoots, TV-series and movies.[[403]](#cite_note-405) The local media infrastructure remains primarily in Johannesburg.\n\n### Hiking and trails\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=79 \"Edit section: Hiking and trails\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/95/P1000280_Table_Mountain_from_slopes_of_Lion%27s_Head.jpg/250px-P1000280_Table_Mountain_from_slopes_of_Lion%27s_Head.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:P1000280_Table_Mountain_from_slopes_of_Lion's_Head.jpg)\n\n[Lion's Head](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lion's_Head_(Cape_Town) \"Lion's Head (Cape Town)\") hike, with [Table Mountain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_Mountain \"Table Mountain\") in the background\n\n [![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0f/Bah%C3%ADa_de_Smitswinkel%2C_Sud%C3%A1frica%2C_2018-07-23%2C_DD_49.jpg/250px-Bah%C3%ADa_de_Smitswinkel%2C_Sud%C3%A1frica%2C_2018-07-23%2C_DD_49.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Bahía_de_Smitswinkel,_Sudáfrica,_2018-07-23,_DD_49.jpg)\n\nSmitswinkel Bay, between [Simon's Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simon's_Town \"Simon's Town\") and [Cape Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Point \"Cape Point\")\n\nCape Town is home to an array of scenic [nature trails](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Footpath \"Footpath\") and [hiking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hiking \"Hiking\") routes, which are frequented by locals. Various trails offer options for walking, running, hiking, climbing, cycling, and [dog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dog \"Dog\")-walking. Trails in public parks are maintained directly by the [City of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Cape_Town \"City of Cape Town\"), while many hiking trails are inside areas that are under [SANParks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SANParks \"SANParks\")' jurisdiction.\n\nPopular hiking routes include Lion's Head, Kalk Bay Peak (in Silvermine), Elsie's Peak, Constantia Nek, the Pipe Track, Platteklip Gorge, Maclear's Beacon, Devil's Peak, Kasteelspoort, Tranquillity Cracks, Kloof Corner, Newlands Forest, Skeleton Gorge, Orange Kloof, Noordhoek Panorama Circuit, Noordhoek Peak, Chapman's Peak, Lighthouse Keeper's Trail, Farmer's Cliffs Trail, Tygerberg Nature Reserve, and Blaauwberg Nature Reserve.[[404]](#cite_note-The_21_Best_Hiking_Trails_in_Cape_Town-406)\n\n### Sport\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=80 \"Edit section: Sport\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/New_stadium_-_panoramio.jpg/250px-New_stadium_-_panoramio.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:New_stadium_-_panoramio.jpg)\n\n[Cape Town Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_Stadium \"Cape Town Stadium\") in [Green Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Point,_Cape_Town \"Green Point, Cape Town\")\n\n [![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bf/Newlands2.jpg/250px-Newlands2.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Newlands2.jpg)\n\n[Newlands Cricket Ground](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newlands_Cricket_Ground \"Newlands Cricket Ground\")\n\n [![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/Newlands_stadium_cropped.jpg/250px-Newlands_stadium_cropped.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Newlands_stadium_cropped.jpg)\n\n[Newlands Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newlands_Stadium \"Newlands Stadium\")\n\nCape Town's most popular sports by participation are [cricket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cricket \"Cricket\"), [soccer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Association_football \"Association football\"), [swimming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swimming_(sport) \"Swimming (sport)\"), and [rugby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rugby_union \"Rugby union\").[[405]](#cite_note-407) In rugby union, Cape Town is the home of the [Western Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Province_(rugby) \"Western Province (rugby)\") side, who play at [Cape Town Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_Stadium \"Cape Town Stadium\") and compete in the [Currie Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Currie_Cup \"Currie Cup\").\n\nIn addition, Western Province players (along with some from Wellington's [Boland Cavaliers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boland_Cavaliers \"Boland Cavaliers\")) form the [Stormers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stormers \"Stormers\") in the [United Rugby Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Rugby_Championship \"United Rugby Championship\") competition. Cape Town has also been a host city for both the [1995 Rugby World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1995_Rugby_World_Cup \"1995 Rugby World Cup\") and [2010 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_FIFA_World_Cup \"2010 FIFA World Cup\"), and annually hosts the Africa leg of the [World Rugby 7s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Rugby_Sevens_Series \"World Rugby Sevens Series\").[[406]](#cite_note-408) Cape Town also hosted the [2023 Netball World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Netball_World_Cup \"2023 Netball World Cup\").[[407]](#cite_note-409)\n\n[Association football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Association_football \"Association football\"), which is mostly known as *soccer* in South Africa, is also popular. [Cape Town City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_City_F.C._(2016) \"Cape Town City F.C. (2016)\") played in the top tier [Premiership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_Premiership \"South African Premiership\") until their relegation at the end of the [2024–25 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024–25_South_African_Premiership \"2024–25 South African Premiership\"). A second major club, [Cape Town Spurs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_Spurs_F.C. \"Cape Town Spurs F.C.\"), were relegated from the second tier [National First Division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_First_Division \"National First Division\") at the end of the [2024–25 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024–25_National_First_Division \"2024–25 National First Division\").\n\nCape Town also hosted several the matches in the [2010 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_FIFA_World_Cup \"2010 FIFA World Cup\") including a semi-final.[[408]](#cite_note-southafrica.info-410) In preparation for the World Cup, a new stadium ([Cape Town Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_Stadium \"Cape Town Stadium\")) was constructed in Green Point.\n\nIn [cricket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cricket \"Cricket\"), the [Cape Cobras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Cobras \"Cape Cobras\") represent Cape Town at the [Newlands Cricket Ground](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newlands_Cricket_Ground \"Newlands Cricket Ground\"). The team is the result of an amalgamation of the [Western Province Cricket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Province_Cricket \"Western Province Cricket\") and [Boland Cricket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boland_Cricket \"Boland Cricket\") teams. They take part in the [Supersport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supersport_Series \"Supersport Series\") and [Standard Bank Cup Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standard_Bank_Cup_Series \"Standard Bank Cup Series\"). The Newlands Cricket Ground regularly hosts international matches.\n\nCape Town has had Olympic aspirations. For example, in 1996, Cape Town was one of the five candidate cities shortlisted by the [IOC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IOC \"IOC\") to launch official candidatures to host the [2004 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004_Summer_Olympics \"2004 Summer Olympics\"). Although the Games ultimately went to [Athens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athens \"Athens\"), Cape Town came in third place.\n\nThere has been some speculation that Cape Town was seeking the South African Olympic Committee's nomination to be South Africa's bid city for the [2020 Summer Olympic Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Summer_Olympic_Games \"2020 Summer Olympic Games\").[[409]](#cite_note-411) That was quashed when the [International Olympic Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Olympic_Committee \"International Olympic Committee\") awarded the 2020 Games to Tokyo.\n\nThe city of Cape Town has vast experience in hosting major national and international sports events. The [Cape Town Cycle Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_Cycle_Tour \"Cape Town Cycle Tour\") is the world's largest individually timed [road cycling race](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Road_bicycle_racing \"Road bicycle racing\") – and the first event outside Europe to be included in the [International Cycling Union's Golden Bike series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UCI_Golden_Bike \"UCI Golden Bike\"). It sees over 35,000 cyclists tackling a 109 km (68 mi) route around Cape Town.\n\nThe [Absa Cape Epic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Absa_Cape_Epic \"Absa Cape Epic\") is the largest full-service mountain bike stage race in the world. Some notable events hosted by Cape Town have included the [1995 Rugby World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1995_Rugby_World_Cup \"1995 Rugby World Cup\"), [2003 ICC Cricket World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2003_ICC_Cricket_World_Cup \"2003 ICC Cricket World Cup\"), and World Championships in various sports such as athletics, fencing, weightlifting, hockey, cycling, canoeing, gymnastics and others.\n\nCape Town was also a host city to the [2010 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_FIFA_World_Cup \"2010 FIFA World Cup\") from 11 June to 11 July 2010, further enhancing its profile as a major events city. It was also one of the host cities of the [2009 Indian Premier League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Indian_Premier_League \"2009 Indian Premier League\") cricket tournament.\n\nThe Mother City has also played host to the [Africa leg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa_Sevens \"South Africa Sevens\") of the annual [World Rugby 7s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Rugby_Sevens_Series \"World Rugby Sevens Series\") event since 2015; for nine seasons, from 2002 until 2010, the event was staged in [George](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George,_South_Africa \"George, South Africa\") in the Western Cape, before moving to [Port Elizabeth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Elizabeth \"Port Elizabeth\") for the [2011 edition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_South_Africa_Sevens \"2011 South Africa Sevens\"), and then to Cape Town in 2015. The event usually takes place in mid-December, and is hosted at the [Cape Town Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_Stadium \"Cape Town Stadium\") in Green Point.[[410]](#cite_note-412)\n\nThere are several golf courses in Cape Town. The Clovelly Country Club and Metropolitan Golf Club have 18 holes.[[411]](#cite_note-413)\n\n### Water-based activities\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=81 \"Edit section: Water-based activities\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b3/Kitesurfers-001.JPG/250px-Kitesurfers-001.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Kitesurfers-001.JPG)\n\n[Kitesurfing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kitesurfing \"Kitesurfing\") in [Table Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_Bay \"Table Bay\")\n\nThe coastline of Cape Town is relatively long, and the varied exposure to weather conditions makes it fairly common for water conditions to be conducive to recreational scuba diving at some part of the city's coast. There is considerable variation in the underwater environment and regional ecology as there are dive sites on reefs and wrecks on both sides of the Cape Peninsula and False Bay, split between two coastal marine ecoregions by the Cape Peninsula, and also variable by depth zone.\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e3/Blue_Wall_of_Sandy_Bay.jpg/250px-Blue_Wall_of_Sandy_Bay.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Blue_Wall_of_Sandy_Bay.jpg)\n\nSurfing in [Sandy Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandy_Bay,_Cape_Town \"Sandy Bay, Cape Town\")\n\nFalse Bay is open to the south, and the prevailing open ocean swell arrives from the southwest, so the exposure varies considerably around the coastline. The inshore bathymetry near Cape Point is shallow enough for a moderate amount of refraction of long period swell, but deep enough to have less effect on short period swell, and acts as a filter to pass mainly the longer swell components to the Western shores, although they are significantly attenuated.\n\nThe eastern shores get more of the open ocean spectrum, and this results in very different swell conditions between the two sides at any given time. The fetch is generally too short for southeasterly winds to produce good surf. There are more than 20 [named breaks in False Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/False_Bay#Surfing \"False Bay\").\n\nThe north-wester can have a long fetch and can produce large waves, but they may also be associated with local wind and be very poorly sorted. The Atlantic coast is exposed to the full power of the South-westerly swell produced by the westerly winds of the southern ocean, often a long way away, so the swell has time to separate into similar wavelengths, and there are some world class big wave breaks among the named breaks of the Atlantic shore.[[412]](#cite_note-Wavescape_1-414)[[413]](#cite_note-Wavescape_2-415)[[414]](#cite_note-Wavescape_3-416)\n\nFurther information on the racing sailboat built in Cape Town: [Cape 31](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_31 \"Cape 31\")\n\nIn the 1950s the city was part of four editions of the famous [Algiers-Cape Town Rally](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algiers-Cape_Town_Rally \"Algiers-Cape Town Rally\"), during the pioneer years of trans-Africa rallying and motor exploration. The closing of the road to the newly founded [Zaire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zaire \"Zaire\") caused the city to be dropped from the traject.[[415]](#cite_note-417)\n\nFurther information on Water-based activities at Zeekoevlei: [Zeekoevlei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zeekoevlei \"Zeekoevlei\")\n\nCape Town is also home to [Zeekoevlei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zeekoevlei \"Zeekoevlei\"), a fresh water lake near the False Bay coast. Its primary use is for sailing and rowing. Zeekoevlei is the home of the *Zeekoevlei Yacht Club*, as well as a hand full of local schools and university rowing clubs, such as [SACS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_College_Schools \"South African College Schools\"), [RBHS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rondebosch_Boys_High_School \"Rondebosch Boys High School\"), [Bishops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bishops_Diocesan_College \"Bishops Diocesan College\"), and [UCT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UCT \"UCT\").\n\n### Libraries\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=82 \"Edit section: Libraries\")]\n\nCape Town is home to 102 public libraries (101 fixed and 1 satellite location), operated directly by the [City of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Cape_Town \"City of Cape Town\"). Visitors can borrow items by registering for a library card.[[416]](#cite_note-Cape_Town_Libraries-418) The city's libraries loan out books, provide access to electronic resources (via free-to-use computers connected to the internet), and facilitate community programs and events.\n\nLocated in Parade Street, close to the Cape Town City Hall, in [Cape Town CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD \"Cape Town CBD\"), [Central Library Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Library_Cape_Town \"Central Library Cape Town\") is a particularly old library. Established in 1954, the library hosts books in sections including children, teens, adult, art, [LGBTQIA+](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQIA+ \"LGBTQIA+\"), and local history. The building also houses around 100 public access computers.[[417]](#cite_note-Cape_Town_Libraries_Tourism-419) The library contains around 200,000 items (including books, magazines, and DVDs), and has around 5,000 weekly visitors.[[418]](#cite_note-The_most_borrowed_books_from_five_Cape_Town_libraries-420)\n\nIn 2025, the City launched a fully-digital library service, which includes access to e-books and an online catalogue that features modern and classic literature. The new system also allows for online application of library cards, to reduce queuing time at physical libraries.[[419]](#cite_note-Cape_Town_libraries_take_a_big_leap_into_the_digital_age-421)\n\n### Museums and theaters\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=83 \"Edit section: Museums and theaters\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/81/Artscape_Theatre_Centre%2C_Cape_Town_%282017%29.jpg/250px-Artscape_Theatre_Centre%2C_Cape_Town_%282017%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Artscape_Theatre_Centre,_Cape_Town_(2017).jpg)\n\nThe main entrance to the [Artscape Theater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artscape_Theatre_Centre \"Artscape Theatre Centre\") in [Cape Town CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD \"Cape Town CBD\")\n\nCape Town has a rich history, and several notable cultural attractions. The [District Six](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_Six \"District Six\") museum provides the public with information about the history of the [Cape Colored](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Coloureds \"Cape Coloureds\") community and the District Six area. The [Iziko South African Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iziko_South_African_Museum \"Iziko South African Museum\"), located in [Cape Town CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD \"Cape Town CBD\"), houses important African zoologic, palaeontologic, and archaeologic collections. Located on the same site as the Iziko Museum is the South African National Gallery.\n\nLocal theaters include the Artscape Theater, Baxter Theater (owned by the University of Cape Town), and the District Six Homecoming Center.\n\n### Car culture\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=84 \"Edit section: Car culture\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1e/Franschhoek_Motor_Museum_on_L%27ormarins_Wine_Farm._-_panoramio.jpg/250px-Franschhoek_Motor_Museum_on_L%27ormarins_Wine_Farm._-_panoramio.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Franschhoek_Motor_Museum_on_L'ormarins_Wine_Farm._-_panoramio.jpg)\n\nThe [Franschhoek Motor Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franschhoek_Motor_Museum \"Franschhoek Motor Museum\"), just outside Cape Town\n\nCape Town has a strong [automotive culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Car_dependency \"Car dependency\"), with numerous annual events and digital communities focused on cars. In-person events include Cars & Coffee, Speed Classic Cape Town, the V&A Waterfront Motorshow, the Classic Car and Bike Show, the Cape Town Motor Show, Things of Beauty, and Cape WheelFest. The city also hosts The Cape 1,000 grand touring rally. The Tannery Car Show takes place in nearby [Wellington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wellington,_South_Africa \"Wellington, South Africa\").\n\nAutomotive [museums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum \"Museum\") in and around the city include the [Franschhoek Motor Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franschhoek_Motor_Museum \"Franschhoek Motor Museum\") in [Franschhoek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franschhoek \"Franschhoek\"), Wijnland Auto Museum in [Kraaifontein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kraaifontein \"Kraaifontein\"),[[420]](#cite_note-Wijnland_Auto_Museum-422) and Tannery Cars, based in Old Tannery, in Wellington.[[421]](#cite_note-Tannery_Cars-423)\n\n### Other\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=85 \"Edit section: Other\")]\n\nWithin [Cape Town CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD \"Cape Town CBD\"), other cultural attractions include the [Houses of Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houses_of_Parliament,_Cape_Town \"Houses of Parliament, Cape Town\") (the seat of the South African national government), the Planetarium, and the [Company's Garden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Company's_Garden \"Company's Garden\") (South Africa's oldest park).\n\n## See also\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Cape_Town&action=edit&section=86 \"Edit section: See also\")]\n\n* Africa portal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portal:South_Africa \"Portal:South Africa\")\n\n* [Cape Town Tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_Tourism \"Cape Town Tourism\") – the city's Town suburbs\")\n* [History of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Cape_Town \"History of Cape Town\")\n* [Timeline of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_Cape_Town \"Timeline of Cape Town\")\n* [City of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Cape_Town \"City of Cape Town\") - the local (metro) government\n\n## Pauline’s\n\nAnother Sea Point coffee shop is Pauline’s, a cafe that strives to connect the growers and roasters with the local community.\n\nI popped in right before closing at 4pm (which I always feel so bad about) and they were incredibly nice. Shift Espresso Bar\n\nShift Espresso Bar has a few locations scattered about town and it is easily one of the best places I had coffee in Cape Town.\n\nEstablished in 2014, Shift has an entire menu of artisan coffee options, from flat whites to red cappuccinos and beyond.\n\nShift Espresso Bar\n\nShift Espresso Bar\n\n Shift Espresso Bar\n\nShift Espresso Bar flat white\n\nThey also serve a mean iced coffee for those hot, summer days. Plant-based milk options include oat, almond, and macadamia.\n\nIf you show up hungry, they have everything from bowls (salmon, mince, chickpeas, and more) to bagels with a variety of toppings. I visited their Kloof Street location but they all have great reviews and deserve a stop if you’re in the city!       "raw_content": "[Skip to content](#main)\n\n\n[![Megan Logo Desktop](https://www.meganstarr.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/04/cropped-Megan-Logo-Desktop.png)](https://www.meganstarr.com/)\n\n[![Logo meganstarr](https://www.meganstarr.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/04/Megan-Logo-Mobile.png)](https://www.meganstarr.com/)\n\n\n\n# 12 Delicious Cafes and Coffee Shops in Cape Town (+ Map!)\n\n![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/758a16fec6960539f0b775cd5de123b79a234f21b4afb22e4b1521d200ee107b?s=30&d=mm&r=g)By:Megan Starr  Updated on:\n\n*This site uses affiliate links, meaning that if you purchase through my links, I may earn an affiliate commission, at no extra cost to you*.\n\nLooking for a **good coffee shop in Cape Town?** Don’t worry, the city has hundreds of them. I was so blown away by all the amazing **cafes in Cape Town** that I found myself at different spots every single day of my trip!\n\nWhile this **Cape Town coffee guide** is nowhere near complete (I will be adding to it on my next trip), I did want to share a handful of places that I think are worth adding to your itinerary if you’re heading to the city!\n\n![Plato (one of the best coffee shops in Cape Town)](https://www.meganstarr.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/04/Plato-2.jpg)\n\nPlato (one of the best cafes in Cape Town)\n\nIn this guide, I also included a few cafes with good food and vibes in addition to the delicious coffee.\n\nSo, if a cafe is not solely a place that focuses on specialty coffee, that is why. At the end of the guide, I have included a map of where to find these **Cape Town coffee shops and cafes!**\n\n![Great coffee in Cape Town is not hard to come by!](https://www.meganstarr.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/04/Deluxe-Coffeeworks-Bo-Kaap.jpg)\n\nGreat coffee in Cape Town is not hard to come by!\n\nAlso, note that these cafes are in various parts of Cape Town and the surrounding area (I stayed in Camps Bay, so a few are in that area).\n\nLet me know if there are any **Cape Town cafes** you love that should be added to my coffee bucket list for my next trip!\n\n## Delicious Coffee Shops in Cape Town\n\n### 1. Okja\n\n**Okja** has two locations throughout the city, and what I loved most about this cafe is that it was completely plant-based.\n\nThey had delicious coffee, tea, and matcha (along with fresh juice and other tasty offerings), along with a variety of plant-based milk products (using Italian oats).\n\n![Okja in Camps Bay - one of my favorite Cape Town coffees!](https://www.meganstarr.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/04/Okja-3.jpg)\n\nOkja in Camps Bay – one of my favorite Cape Town coffees!\n\nTheir original Cape Town cafe opened in Camps Bay in 2020, and it eventually gave way to the opening of the Kloof Str. location (where you can also grab a tasty pastry with your coffee).\n\nThey have an ex-Michelin-star pastry chef who is creating the sweet masterpieces they sell, and it sets them apart from other coffee shops in Cape Town!\n\n**📍 Locations:** 77 Kloof St. and Victoria Road (Camps Bay)  \n**🔗 Website:** <https://www.okja.co/>\n\n### 2. Plato Coffee\n\nAnother last-day discovery was **Plato Coffee**. The beautiful cafe in Cape Town was so friendly and welcoming that it ended up being one of my absolute favorite places I visited in the city.\n\nI popped into their Sea Point location only to realize that this coffee shop had a lot of sister cafes across South Africa.\n\n![Plato - amazing coffee shop in Cape Town](https://www.meganstarr.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/04/Plato-3.jpg)\n\nPlato – amazing coffee shop in Cape Town\n\nI guess I will be trying them out in various locations when I get back to South Africa because it was that good.\n\nTheir Sea Point location is at 120 Main Street and is open 7 days a week since opening in August 2023.\n\n**📍 Locations:** several across South Africa  \n**🔗 Website:** <https://plato.coffee/>\n\n### 3. Nøsh Cafe\n\nI would have never discovered **Nøsh Cafe** had I not been seeking a place with immediate air-conditioning where I could cool off.\n\nNøsh is a new cafe located in the Time Out Market at the V&A Waterfront. The cafe showcases the best of Cape Town’s coffee culture as it offers beans from some of the top coffee brands in the country (Truth, Origin, and Tribe are a few). The founder is Joel Singer, who is also the founder of Origin Coffee Roasting.\n\n![Nøsh Coffee in Time Out](https://www.meganstarr.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/04/Nosh.jpg)\n\nNøsh Cafe in Time Out\n\nAt Nøsh, you can order everything from a Chemex to a flat white, and all can be paired with some scrumptious food choices like kimchi cheese steak sandwiches (and more). This is a fantastic place to add to your Cape Town coffee bucket list!\n\n**📍 Location:** The Old Power Station, Dock Rd., V&A Waterfront  \n**🔗 Website:** <https://www.timeoutmarket.com/capetown/eat-and-drink/nosh-cafe/>\n\n### 4. Pauline’s\n\nAnother Sea Point coffee shop is **Pauline’s**, a cafe that strives to connect the growers and roasters with the local community.\n\nI popped in right before closing at 4pm (which I always feel so bad about) and they were incredibly nice. I ordered a standard flat white while my partner ordered a tea.\n\n![Outside Pauline's](https://www.meganstarr.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/04/Paulines-3.jpg)\n\nOutside Pauline’s\n\n ![Coffee at Pauline's](https://www.meganstarr.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/04/Paulines-2.jpg)\n\nCoffee at Pauline’s\n\nThey had run out of what he ordered, as it was at the end of the day, but they (without asking) whipped him up a large drink and gave it to him on the house.\n\nWhat a delightful experience! Cape Town has incredible customer service wherever I went, but Pauline’s was, hands-down, some of the best. Prioritize this cafe, please.\n\n**📍 Location:** 277 Main Rd  \n**🔗 Website:** <https://paulinescoffee.com/>\n\n### 5. Deluxe Coffeeworks\n\n**Deluxe Coffeeworks** was founded in 2009 by Judd Nicolay (from New Zealand) and Carl Wessel.\n\nToday, they have 5 cafes in South Africa, 2 in Namibia, and one in the UK. I had the opportunity to visit their small Bo-Kaap shop and loved it.\n\n![Deluxe Coffeeworks Bo Kaap](https://www.meganstarr.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/04/Deluxe-Coffeeworks-Bo-Kaap-2.jpg)\n\nDeluxe Coffeeworks Bo-Kaap\n\nThe coffee was refreshingly good and they had vintage posters from DRUM Magazine covers on the walls.\n\nI believe there was a recent redesign in the cafe as the photos on Google search don’t look anything like what I walked into (I loved what it looks like now, as it was so fitting for the neighborhood it is in). They are closed on Sundays.\n\n**📍 Location:** 81 Rose Street (Bo-Kaap) but they have many locations  \n**🔗 Website:** <https://www.deluxecoffeeworks.co.za/>\n\n### 6. Our Local\n\n**Our Local** is another brunch cafe where I found super palatable coffee and food. They welcome guests with the motto *“Imagine the perfect brunch in your botanist best friend’s greenhouse,”* and that is exactly what it felt like!\n\nThey specialize in brunch but are now open for dinner from Wednesday to Saturday.\n\n![Our Local coffee](https://www.meganstarr.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/04/Our-Local-3.jpg)\n\nOur Local coffee\n\n ![Our Local - a popular Cape Town cafe](https://www.meganstarr.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/04/Our-Local-4.jpg)\n\nOur Local – a popular Cape Town cafe\n\nI ordered a flat white and shakshuka. I was there around lunchtime, and there was a line out the door, but since I was dining alone, they were able to squeeze me in pretty quickly. Just know that you will be waiting a bit for a table (especially if you’re visiting on the weekend).\n\nOpen from 8am-3:30pm on Sundays and Mondays and 8am until late on the other nights, you can find breakfast items on the menu until 12pm (items like open-faced omelets and anchovy toast).\n\nDuring the afternoon, they serve a few types of shakshuka (including a vegan option), and more.\n\n**📍 Location:** 117 Kloof Str.  \n**🔗 Website:** <https://www.ourlocal.co.za/>\n\n### 7. Naked Kitchen and Coffee Bar\n\n**Naked Coffee** is a Johannesburg coffee shop that opened a location in Sea Point in 2019.\n\nI visited on my last day in Cape Town as I was trying to make up for lost time from the days I was sick, and I popped in right before they were going to close. Therefore, I only tried their coffee.\n\n![Coffee at Naked](https://www.meganstarr.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/04/Naked-2.jpg)\n\nCoffee at Naked\n\nThey had the usual suspects like Americanos, flavored lattes, and flat whites. I opted for a simple flat white and enjoyed every last drop of it.\n\nThe vibe was cool inside the cafe and the staff was exceptionally friendly. I will definitely visit when I am back in Cape Town, as I am curious to taste something from their brunch menu (which contains a lot of egg dishes… my favorite!).\n\n**📍 Location:** 2 Wisbeach Road  \n**🔗 Website:** <https://www.facebook.com/NakedCoffeeWisbeach/>\n\n### 8. Mantra Cafe\n\nMy first breakfast in Cape Town was at **Mantra Cafe** in Camps Bay. They opened at 8:30am and had an array of menu items that all sounded so great after landing so late the night before.\n\n![Mantra Cafe - coffee](https://www.meganstarr.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/04/Mantra-Cafe-4.jpg)\n\nMantra Cafe – coffee\n\nMantra Cafe offers sea views, local homemade gifts, and fabulous food and coffee options.\n\n![Inside Mantra Cafe in Camps Bay](https://www.meganstarr.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/04/Mantra-Cafe-2.jpg)\n\nInside Mantra Cafe in Camps Bay\n\nEd, my partner, ordered avocado toast while I opted for their marrow and potato hashbrowns topped with kale, tomatoes, a poached egg, and dukkah.\n\nMy flat white was made to perfection and was the ideal start to a day exploring the streets of Cape Town. They are open daily from 8:30am until 10:00pm.\n\n**📍 Location:** 43 Victoria Road (Camps Bay)  \n**🔗 Website:** <https://www.mantracafe.co.za/>\n\n### 9. Jarryds Brunch & Bistro\n\nI had planned to visit **Jarryds Brunch & Bistro** for a sit-down breakfast later in my trip (I ended up sick, sadly), but I am glad I had a chance to visit and at least grab a to-go coffee while there!\n\n![Creating the perfect coffee](https://www.meganstarr.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/04/Jarryds.jpg)\n\nCreating the perfect coffee\n\n ![Yep- was perfect!](https://www.meganstarr.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/04/Jarryds-2.jpg)\n\nYep- was perfect!\n\nI walked away with a flat white (and Ed took an English breakfast tea), and it was delicious! In addition to good coffee, Jarryds has various brunch choices like fluffy pancake stacks, gyros bowls, and more.\n\nThey are open from 7:30am until 4pm daily and have a diverse menu. This is a top spot if you’re strolling around Sea Point.\n\n**📍 Location:** 90 Regent Rd  \n**🔗 Website:** <https://www.facebook.com/JARRYDSEATERY/>\n\n### 10. Shift Espresso Bar\n\n**Shift Espresso Bar** has a few locations scattered about town and it is easily one of the best places I had coffee in Cape Town.\n\nEstablished in 2014, Shift has an entire menu of artisan coffee options, from flat whites to red cappuccinos and beyond.\n\n![Shift Espresso Bar](https://www.meganstarr.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/04/Shift-Espresso-Bar.jpg)\n\nShift Espresso Bar\n\n ![Shift Espresso Bar](https://www.meganstarr.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/04/Shift-Espresso-Bar-2.jpg)\n\nShift Espresso Bar flat white\n\nThey also serve a mean iced coffee for those hot, summer days. Plant-based milk options include oat, almond, and macadamia.\n\nIf you show up hungry, they have everything from bowls (salmon, mince, chickpeas, and more) to bagels with a variety of toppings. I visited their Kloof Street location but they all have great reviews and deserve a stop if you’re in the city!\n\nThey are open daily from 6:30am until 6pm daily except on Sundays when they are open from 7am until 3pm.\n\n**📍 Locations:** there are 11 locations, and you can find them on their website below  \n**🔗 Website:** <https://www.shiftespresso.com/>\n\n### 11. The Pottery Camps Bay\n\nWith five locations nationwide, **The Pottery** was a one-of-a-kind coffee experience in Cape Town. Their [Camps Bay location](https://www.meganstarr.com/camps-bay-things-to-do/) opened in December 2021 and has been a hit with locals ever since.\n\nThey have a limited food and beverage menu (but the pizza and coffee are SO good!) and sell Wonki Ware crockery and other pottery pieces.\n\n![The Pottery Camps Bay](https://www.meganstarr.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/04/The-Pottery-Camps-Bay-3.jpg)\n\nThe Pottery Camps Bay\n\n ![The Pottery Camps Bay (flat white)](https://www.meganstarr.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/04/The-Pottery-Camps-Bay-4.jpg)\n\nThe Pottery Camps Bay (flat white)\n\nThe Pottery also offers the opportunity to design and paint your own ceramics in-house, which is super unique and something great for both kids and adults.\n\n**📍 Location:** 41 The Drive, Camps Bay  \n**🔗 Website:** <https://campsbay.thepottery.co.za/>\n\n### 12. Bootlegger Coffee Company\n\n**Bootlegger Coffee Company** has locations all over Cape Town, but they don’t seem to compromise quality despite being ever-present.\n\nThe coffee shop was started in 2012 by three friends and it has expanded all over the city and even up to Johannesburg! The one I went to was in Camps Bay, and it was one of their new pop-ups.\n\n![Bootlegger pop up in Camps Bay](https://www.meganstarr.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/04/Bootlegger-3.jpg)\n\nBootlegger pop up in Camps Bay\n\nAnd by ‘I went there’, I didn’t. I was super sick, and Ed went and grabbed me a coffee from there, as I had it on my list to try before leaving.\n\n![Bootlegger](https://www.meganstarr.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/04/Bootlegger.jpg)\n\nThanks for spilling the coffee, Ed\n\nThe coffee was heavenly and did just the trick. They roast their beans in Ndabeni, Cape Town on Giesens, and they also offer food with local produce at locations. Bootlegger is a great choice if you’re looking for consistently good coffee in Cape Town.\n\n**📍 Locations:** many; check their website!  \n**🔗 Website:** <https://www.bootlegger.coffee/>\n\n## Cape Town Cafes: On a Map!\n\nI have included a map of the locations I went to for every place. Please note that many of these places have multiple locations (I noted that above).\n\n#### How To Use This Map\n\nYou can use your fingers/mouse to zoom in and out. To get more info about a place, simply touch/click the icons. Want to save this map for later use? Click the ‘⭐’ by the map title and it will add it to your Google Maps account (Saved > Maps or ‘Your Places’).\n\nAre there any **Cape Town cafes** that should be included on this list? Let me know in the comments. Thanks!\n\n## More Cape Town Travel Guides\n\n* [Snorkeling with seals in Cape Town](https://www.meganstarr.com/snorkeling-seals-cape-town-tour/)\n* [Best beaches in Cape Town](https://www.meganstarr.com/beaches-cape-town/)\n* [Tidal pools in Cape Town, South Africa](https://www.meganstarr.com/tidal-pools-cape-town/)\n* [What to do in Camps Bay](https://www.meganstarr.com/camps-bay-things-to-do/)\n* [Best Cape Town cooking class](https://www.meganstarr.com/cape-malay-cooking-class-review/)\n\n## Pin this Cape Town Coffee Guide\n\n![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/758a16fec6960539f0b775cd5de123b79a234f21b4afb22e4b1521d200ee107b?s=80&d=mm&r=g)\n\n**Megan Starr**\n\nMegan is a travel blogger and writer with a background in digital marketing. Originally from Richmond, VA, she now lives in Finnish Lapland after previous stints in Norway, Germany, Armenia, and Kazakhstan. She has a passion for winter travel, as well as the Nordic countries, but you can also find her eating her way through Italy, perusing perfume stores in Paris, or taking road trips through the USA. Megan has written for or been featured by National Geographic, Forbes, Lonely Planet, the New York Times, and more. She co-authored Fodor's Travel 'Essential Norway' (2020) and has visited 45 US states and 100+ countries.\n\n### Leave a Reply [Cancel reply](/cape-town-coffee-shops-cafes/#respond)\n\n ",
